## Acts 1:1–3 (id: 9275)

Here, Luke summarizes his first book, the Gospel of Luke, which he wrote for Theophilus. Luke also calls him “most excellent” ([Luke 1:3](https://ref.ly/Luke1:3)). This title indicates that he was a person of very important social status (compare [Acts 23:26](https://ref.ly/Acts23:26); [24:2](https://ref.ly/Acts24:2); [26:25](https://ref.ly/Acts26:25)). He was probably a financial donor or patron who helped Luke with the costs of publishing or distributing his work (see study note on Luke 1:3).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 1:1-3
* **Associated Articles:** Luke 1:3 (ID: `20435@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Theophilus (ID: `person:Theophilus`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`)
## Acts 1:3 (id: 9287)

* *He presented Himself to them with many convincing proofs that He was alive*: The Gospels provide evidence for Christ’s resurrection:

    + Jesus’s tomb was empty ([Luke 24:3–4](https://ref.ly/Luke24:3-Luke24:4)).
        + No one disturbed his grave clothes ([John 20:3–9](https://ref.ly/John20:3-John20:9)).
        + Many people saw him (see [Matthew 28](https://ref.ly/Matt28:1-Matt28:20); [Mark 16:1–8](https://ref.ly/Mark16:1-Mark16:8); [Luke 24:1–53](https://ref.ly/Luke24:1-Luke24:53); [John 20:11–21:23](https://ref.ly/John20:11-John21:23); [1 Corinthians 15:3–8](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:3-1Cor15:8)).
        + Jesus appeared to the apostles to help them overcome their doubt ([Matthew 28:17](https://ref.ly/Matt28:17); [Luke 24:17–24](https://ref.ly/Luke24:17-Luke24:24), [38](https://ref.ly/Luke24:38), [41](https://ref.ly/Luke24:41); [John 20:27](https://ref.ly/John20:27); see [Mark 16:14](https://ref.ly/Mark16:14)).
* *the kingdom of God*: This was the main theme of Jesus’s teaching (see[Acts 1:6–8](https://ref.ly/Acts1:6-Acts1:8); [Matthew 4:17](https://ref.ly/Matt4:17); [Mark 1:14–15](https://ref.ly/Mark1:14-Mark1:15); [Luke 4:43](https://ref.ly/Luke4:43)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 1:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Acts 1:6–11 (id: 9290)

*He was taken up:* Jesusascended into heaven (see also [Luke 24:50–53](https://ref.ly/Luke24:50-Luke24:53)). This happened on the Mount of Olives ([Acts 1:12](https://ref.ly/Acts1:12); [Luke 24:50](https://ref.ly/Luke24:50)). This was the last physical appearance of Jesus. He ascended into heaven, where he will stay "until the time comes for the restoration of all things" ([Acts 3:21](https://ref.ly/Acts3:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 1:6-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Witness (ID: `keyterm:Witness`); Galileans (ID: `group:Galilee`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`)
## Acts 1:8 (id: 9291)

* This key verse shows how the good news about Jesus extended from place to place:

    + It began in Jerusalem and Judea ([1:6–8:1](https://ref.ly/Acts1:6-Acts8:1)).
        + It then came to Samaria ([8:4–25](https://ref.ly/Acts8:4-Acts8:25)).
        + Next, it went to Antioch in Syria ([11:19–30](https://ref.ly/Acts11:19-Acts11:30)).
        + Later, it extended across the Mediterranean Sea to Rome ([13:1–28:31](https://ref.ly/Acts13:1-Acts28:31)).
* Christ gave a similar mission to Saul of Tarsus ([9:15](https://ref.ly/Acts9:15)). At first, Saul was well\-known for persecuting Jewish Christians. After his encounter with Christ, he became the main person spreading the gospel to non\-Jews (gentiles).
* *when the Holy Spirit comes: The Holy Spirit* provided the power for all these missionary efforts for the good news about Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 1:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Witness (ID: `keyterm:Witness`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`)

All eleven of the remaining apostles were present (compare [Matthew 10:2–4](https://ref.ly/Matt10:2-Matt10:4); [Mark 3:16–19](https://ref.ly/Mark3:16-Mark3:19); [Luke 6:13–16](https://ref.ly/Luke6:13-Luke6:16)). ## Acts 1:14 (id: 9281)

* The first disciples came together for worship, fellowship, and prayer (see also [2:42](https://ref.ly/Acts2:42), [46–47](https://ref.ly/Acts2:46-Acts2:47); [4:24–31](https://ref.ly/Acts4:24-Acts4:31); [5:12](https://ref.ly/Acts5:12); [12:5](https://ref.ly/Acts12:5); [13:1–3](https://ref.ly/Acts13:1-Acts13:3)). ## Acts 1:18–19 (id: 9283)

The account in the Gospel of Matthew is slightly different ([Matthew 27:3–10](https://ref.ly/Matt27:3-Matt27:10)). We can match the two accounts by understanding the priests' purchase of the “Field of Blood” as if they acted on behalf of Judas. Also, Judas may have killed himself by hanging, but he later fell headfirst. Then his body split open, possibly when the rope broke.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 1:18-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Akeldama (ID: `place:Akeldama`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Unjust Deed (ID: `keyterm:UnjustDeed`)
## Acts 1:20–21 (id: 9284)

*May another take his position*: It was extremely important to find someone to replace Judas so there would still be twelve apostles. The church was the new Israel so it needed twelve leaders chosen and appointed by God (see [Matthew 19:28](https://ref.ly/Matt19:28); [Luke 22:29–30](https://ref.ly/Luke22:29-Luke22:30)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 1:20-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Position of Responsibility (ID: `keyterm:PositionOfResponsibility`)
## Acts 1:21–22 (id: 9285)

To qualify to be an apostle, a man needed to have been with the other apostles throughout the public ministry of Jesus. This period started from the time John baptized Jesus and continued until Jesus was taken up into heaven.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 1:21-22

## Acts 1:23–26 (id: 9286)

* With prayer and humble dependence on God that he would reveal his will, the apostles cast lots ([1:14](https://ref.ly/Acts1:14), [24](https://ref.ly/Acts1:24)). This was a traditional way to discover God’s will (see [Leviticus 16:8](https://ref.ly/Lev16:8); [Numbers 27:21](https://ref.ly/Num27:21); [Deuteronomy 33:8](https://ref.ly/Deut33:8); [Joshua 14:2](https://ref.ly/Josh14:2); [18:3–10](https://ref.ly/Josh18:3-Josh18:10); [Proverbs 16:33](https://ref.ly/Prov16:33)). God chose Matthias by lot to replace Judas. After Pentecost, the Holy Spirit guided the followers of Jesus through dreams, visions, and prophecies instead of through lots (compare [Acts 2:17–18](https://ref.ly/Acts2:17-Acts2:18); [13:2](https://ref.ly/Acts13:2); [16:7–10](https://ref.ly/Acts16:7-Acts16:10)).
* *You know everyone's heart*: See also [1 Samuel 16:6–7](https://ref.ly/1Sam16:6-1Sam16:7); [1 Kings 8:39](https://ref.ly/1Kgs8:39); [1 Chronicles 28:9](https://ref.ly/1Chr28:9); [2 Chronicles 6:30](https://ref.ly/2Chr6:30); [Psalms 7:9](https://ref.ly/Ps7:9); [44:21](https://ref.ly/Ps44:21); [Jeremiah 11:20](https://ref.ly/Jer11:20); [John 2:24–25](https://ref.ly/John2:24-John2:25); [Revelation 2:23](https://ref.ly/Rev2:23).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 1:23-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Matthias (ID: `person:Matthias`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.9`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`); Knower of Hearts (ID: `keyterm:KnowerOfHearts`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Judas (ID: `person:Judas.2`); Lot (ID: `realia:Lot`)
## Acts 2:1–4 (id: 9449)

Jews observed three traditional annual festivals. Many people traveled to the temple in Jerusalem for these feasts (see [Leviticus 23:1–44](https://ref.ly/Lev23:1-Lev23:44)): 

* The Feast of Unleavened Bread (which includes Passover)
* The Feast of Weeks (also called Pentecost or the Feast of Harvest)
* The Feast of Tabernacles

On Pentecost, Jewish people gathered in Jerusalem to thank God for the harvest and his blessings. At the same time, the disciples of Jesus gathered together in prayer with shared hope. Then the Holy Spirit came and filled the believers who were present.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 2:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)
## Acts 2:4 (id: 9460)

* *And they were all filled with the Holy Spirit*: This event marks when the Holy Spirit came down from heaven. He filled the disciples, as Jesus had promised, to empower them as witnesses of Christ's resurrection ([1:4–5](https://ref.ly/Acts1:4-Acts1:5), [8](https://ref.ly/Acts1:8)). The Spirit's wisdom, energy, and power enabled the church's work and witness (for example, [2:14–41](https://ref.ly/Acts2:14-Acts2:41), [43](https://ref.ly/Acts2:43); [4:31](https://ref.ly/Acts4:31); [9:17](https://ref.ly/Acts9:17), [20](https://ref.ly/Acts9:20); [13:9–12](https://ref.ly/Acts13:9-Acts13:12); see also [Ephesians 5:18](https://ref.ly/Eph5:18)).
* *began to speak in other tongues*: The Holy Spirit gave them the ability to communicate so people from other countries could hear about God's works in their own languages ([Acts 2:6–11](https://ref.ly/Acts2:6-Acts2:11)). This passage is one of several references to speaking in "other tongues" (see also [10:44–48](https://ref.ly/Acts10:44-Acts10:48); [19:6](https://ref.ly/Acts19:6); [1 Corinthians 14:2–28](https://ref.ly/1Cor14:2-1Cor14:28), [39](https://ref.ly/1Cor14:39)). This divine gift shows the energy of the presence of God's Spirit. This event marks the beginning of the gospel (the good news about Jesus) being announced to people from every nation.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 2:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`)
## Acts 2:5–11 (id: 9461)

Since Pentecost was a feast people had to travel to attend, Jews from every nation were in Jerusalem (see study note on 2:1–4). They could hear the gospel (the good news about Jesus) in their own languages. The gospel is for all people ([2:38–39](https://ref.ly/Acts2:38-Acts2:39)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 2:5-11
* **Associated Articles:** Acts 2:1-4 (ID: `9449@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Phrygia (ID: `place:Phrygia`); Medan (ID: `person:Medan`); Galileans (ID: `group:Galilee`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Libya (ID: `place:Libya`); Elamites (ID: `group:Elam`); Pontus (ID: `place:Pontus`); Elam (ID: `place:Elam`); Cappadocia (ID: `place:Cappadocia`); Parthians (ID: `group:Parthians`); Cretans (ID: `group:Cretan`); Cyrene (ID: `place:Cyrene`); Arabians (ID: `group:Arabia`); Medes (ID: `group:Mede`); Pamphylia (ID: `place:Pamphylia`); Asia (ID: `place:Asia`); Crete (ID: `place:Crete`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Mighty Act (ID: `keyterm:MightyAct`); Mesopotamia (ID: `place:Mesopotamia`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Rome (ID: `place:Rome`); Arabia (ID: `place:Arabia`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)
## Acts 2:9–11 (id: 9462)

* The Parthians came from the region between the Tigris River and India.

    The Medes, now known as Kurds, were from Media, located east of Mesopotamia and north of the Persian Gulf.

    The Elamites were from Elam. This area is now in Iran, north of the Persian Gulf and just east of the Tigris River.

    Mesopotamia was between the Tigris and Euphrates rivers.

    Judea was well known as the Jewish homeland.

    Cappadocia, Pontus, and Asia were Roman provinces in Asia Minor, now part of Turkey (see [1 Peter 1:1](https://ref.ly/1Pet1:1)). The apostle Paul later taught and preached in some of these areas (see [Acts 16:6](https://ref.ly/Acts16:6); [19:10](https://ref.ly/Acts19:10), [26](https://ref.ly/Acts19:26)).

    Phrygia and Pamphylia were districts in Asia Minor that Paul later visited ([13:13](https://ref.ly/Acts13:13); [18:23](https://ref.ly/Acts18:23)).

    Egypt had a large Jewish population, especially in Alexandria (see [18:24](https://ref.ly/Acts18:24)).

    North Africa included Libya and Cyrene.

    Rome was the capital of the Roman Empire and home to thousands of *Jews*.

    The Cretans were from the island of Crete, located south\-southeast of Greece.

    The Arabs were from the area south and east of Jerusalem.

* Including all of these nations demonstrates how God intended the gospel to reach the ends of the earth ([1:8](https://ref.ly/Acts1:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 2:9-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Phrygia (ID: `place:Phrygia`); Medan (ID: `person:Medan`); Libya (ID: `place:Libya`); Elamites (ID: `group:Elam`); Pontus (ID: `place:Pontus`); Elam (ID: `place:Elam`); Cretans (ID: `group:Cretan`); Cappadocia (ID: `place:Cappadocia`); Parthians (ID: `group:Parthians`); Cyrene (ID: `place:Cyrene`); Arabians (ID: `group:Arabia`); Medes (ID: `group:Mede`); Pamphylia (ID: `place:Pamphylia`); Asia (ID: `place:Asia`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Crete (ID: `place:Crete`); Mighty Act (ID: `keyterm:MightyAct`); Mesopotamia (ID: `place:Mesopotamia`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Rome (ID: `place:Rome`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Arabia (ID: `place:Arabia`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

## Acts 2:14–36 (id: 9451)

This is the first of about 30 speeches in Acts and one of the most important. It marks the beginning of the Christian church. It is a typical example of the preaching of the apostles. 

The apostles proclaimed: 

1. that the Old Testament promises came true in the life, death, and resurrection of Jesus, the promised Messiah;
2. that the apostles were eyewitnesses of the public ministry of Jesus and were his chosen representatives;
3. that people must turn away (repent) from their sins and trust in God through Christ; and
4. that salvation and the presence of the Holy Spirit are promised to those who have a positive response to this message of the gospel.

This basic message repeats in the sermons of chapters [3](https://ref.ly/Acts3:1-Acts3:26), [4](https://ref.ly/Acts4:1-Acts4:37), [5](https://ref.ly/Acts5:1-Acts5:42), [8](https://ref.ly/Acts8:1-Acts8:40), [10](https://ref.ly/Acts10:1-Acts10:48), [and 13](https://ref.ly/Acts13:1-Acts13:52). The same themes characterized the apostle Paul's preaching (see [1 Corinthians 15:3–9](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:3-1Cor15:9)). The apostles all repeated this message when preaching to both Jews and non\-Jews (Gentiles) throughout the world around the Mediterranean Sea. All people must turn away (repent) from their sins and turn to God by trusting in Jesus Christ.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 2:14-36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); David (ID: `person:David`); Portent (ID: `keyterm:Portent`); World of the Dead (ID: `keyterm:WorldOfTheDead`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Speak as a Prophet (ID: `keyterm:Speak`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Call Upon (ID: `keyterm:CallUpon`); Joel (ID: `person:Joel.14`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Judeans (ID: `group:Judea`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Promise (ID: `keyterm:Promise`); Something Definite (ID: `keyterm:SomethingDefinite`); Oath (ID: `keyterm:Oath`); Israelites (ID: `group:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Nazareth (ID: `place:Nazareth`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Lawless (ID: `keyterm:Lawless`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`); Witness (ID: `keyterm:Witness`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Throne (ID: `realia:Throne`); Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`); Footstool (ID: `realia:Footstool`)
## Acts 2:17–21 (id: 9452)

This passage quotes [Joel 2:28–32](https://ref.ly/Joel2:28-Joel2:32). The prophet Joel prophesied that in the last days, many people would have the gifts of prophecy, including visions and dreams. This would include both young and old, and both men and women ([Joel 2:28–29](https://ref.ly/Joel2:28-Joel2:29)). In Acts, on the day of Pentecost, Peter announced that Joel’s prophecy was coming true, indicating that "the last days" had begun.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 2:17-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Speak as a Prophet (ID: `keyterm:Speak`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Call Upon (ID: `keyterm:CallUpon`); Portent (ID: `keyterm:Portent`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)
## Acts 2:23–28 (id: 9453)

Although cruel men put Jesus to death on a cross, they did not defeat God’s plan for salvation. In fact, God raised Jesus from the dead, just as David had foretold ([2:25–28](https://ref.ly/Acts2:25-Acts2:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 2:23-28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); World of the Dead (ID: `keyterm:WorldOfTheDead`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Lawless (ID: `keyterm:Lawless`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); David (ID: `person:David`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)
## Acts 2:27 (id: 9454)

*abandon my soul to Hades*: See the study note on Matthew 11:23.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 2:27
* **Associated Articles:** Matthew 11:23 (ID: `21692@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** World of the Dead (ID: `keyterm:WorldOfTheDead`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`)
## Acts 2:32–36 (id: 9455)

*God has raised this Jesus to life, to which we are all witnesses*: The theme of witnessing repeats often in Acts (for example, [3:15](https://ref.ly/Acts3:15); [4:33](https://ref.ly/Acts4:33); [5:32](https://ref.ly/Acts5:32); [10:39–43](https://ref.ly/Acts10:39-Acts10:43)). The main point of this message is that God the Father, as he promised, is now pouring out his Holy Spirit on the church through the resurrected Jesus. All the people of Israel were called to recognize that God had acted with power. He made Jesus, whom they put to death on a cross, both Lord and Messiah!

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 2:32-36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Promise (ID: `keyterm:Promise`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Something Definite (ID: `keyterm:SomethingDefinite`); Witness (ID: `keyterm:Witness`); David (ID: `person:David`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`); Footstool (ID: `realia:Footstool`)

This passage quotes [Psalm 110:1](https://ref.ly/Ps110:1), which Jesus fulfilled when he ascended (went up) into heaven ([Acts 1:9](https://ref.ly/Acts1:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 2:34-35

* **Associated Articles:** Baptism (ID: `500380@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

## Acts 2:42 (id: 9459)

* *fellowship*: The Greek word *koinōnia* means a close relationship marked by sharing and participating in life together.
* *and to prayer:* At this stage, prayer probably included taking part in the formal prayers of the temple (see [3:1](https://ref.ly/Acts3:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 2:42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fellowship (ID: `keyterm:Fellowship`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`)
## Acts 2:42–47 (id: 9458)

Luke makes a clear connection between personal faith and belonging to the Christian community. Life in this new community included:

* devotion to the teaching of the apostles from God’s word,
* fellowship,
* sharing,
* joy, and
* praise.

As a result, the Lord continued to add to their number those who were being saved.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 2:42-47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fellowship (ID: `keyterm:Fellowship`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Portent (ID: `keyterm:Portent`); In Common (ID: `keyterm:InCommon`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`)
## Acts 3:1–11 (id: 9590)

Jesus promised that his disciples would do greater works than he did ([John 14:12](https://ref.ly/John14:12); see [Mark 16:20](https://ref.ly/Mark16:20)). This promise came true through the signs, wonders, and mighty works of the apostles ([Acts 2:43](https://ref.ly/Acts2:43); [5:12](https://ref.ly/Acts5:12); [8:4–8](https://ref.ly/Acts8:4-Acts8:8)). 

The apostle Peter showed the power to heal in the name of Jesus Christ of Nazareth ([3:6](https://ref.ly/Acts3:6), [16](https://ref.ly/Acts3:16)). The healing was immediate and evident ([3:8](https://ref.ly/Acts3:8)). It led the man to praise God ([3:8–9](https://ref.ly/Acts3:8-Acts3:9); compare [2:47](https://ref.ly/Acts2:47); [16:25](https://ref.ly/Acts16:25); [Luke 2:20](https://ref.ly/Luke2:20); [17:15–18](https://ref.ly/Luke17:15-Luke17:18); [18:43](https://ref.ly/Luke18:43); [19:37](https://ref.ly/Luke19:37); [24:53](https://ref.ly/Luke24:53)). This event is the first of many displays of divine power given to the apostles in Acts ([Acts 4:24–31](https://ref.ly/Acts4:24-Acts4:31); [5:12](https://ref.ly/Acts5:12); [6:8](https://ref.ly/Acts6:8); [8:6](https://ref.ly/Acts8:6); [9:33–42](https://ref.ly/Acts9:33-Acts9:42); [28:8](https://ref.ly/Acts28:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 3:1-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Beautiful Gate (ID: `place:BeautifulGate`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Solomon's Colonnade (ID: `place:SolomonsPortico`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Nazareth (ID: `place:Nazareth`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Porch (ID: `realia:Porch`); Door (ID: `realia:Door`)
## Acts 3:6 (id: 9600)

*In the name of Jesus*: Speaking the name of Jesus demonstrates who he is and his power to heal (see [Mark 9:38–39](https://ref.ly/Mark9:38-Mark9:39)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 3:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Nazareth (ID: `place:Nazareth`)
## Acts 3:10–11 (id: 9591)

* The Beautiful Gate was probably the Nicanor Gate, made of bronze from the city of Corinth. It was on the east side of the temple (see Josephus, *War* 5\.5\.3\). It had rich decorations of silver and gold plates on its panels. The gold and silver were heavier and more valuable than the plates on other gates.
* Solomon’s Colonnade was a covered place to walk on the east side of the temple (see Josephus, *War* 5\.5\.1–3; *Antiquities* 20\.9\.7\).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 3:10-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Solomon's Colonnade (ID: `place:SolomonsPortico`); Beautiful Gate (ID: `place:BeautifulGate`); Porch (ID: `realia:Porch`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)
## Acts 3:12–26 (id: 9592)

The speeches in the book of Acts about telling others the good news focus on Jesus, who was put to death on a cross and raised to life. These speeches call people to repent and to place their faith in Jesus as the promised Messiah and the judge God has appointed ([2:38](https://ref.ly/Acts2:38); [3:19](https://ref.ly/Acts3:19); [11:18](https://ref.ly/Acts11:18); [17:30](https://ref.ly/Acts17:30); [26:20](https://ref.ly/Acts26:20)). They also announce the same good news for the people of Israel and for Gentiles everywhere: there is "peace through Jesus Christ, who is Lord of all" ([10:36](https://ref.ly/Acts10:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 3:12-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Samuel (ID: `person:Samuel`); To Cause to Happen (ID: `keyterm:Happen`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Israelites (ID: `group:Israel`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Witness (ID: `keyterm:Witness`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)
## Acts 3:15 (id: 9593)

Many Jewish people could not imagine that God himself, the author of life, could be killed. They did not recognize Jesus as the Messiah (compare [13:27](https://ref.ly/Acts13:27); [Luke 23:34](https://ref.ly/Luke23:34)). They also did not understand that the Messiah would be divine, that is, sharing God’s nature (see [John 1:1–18](https://ref.ly/John1:1-John1:18); compare [Daniel 7:13–14](https://ref.ly/Dan7:13-Dan7:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 3:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Witness (ID: `keyterm:Witness`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Acts 3:17 (id: 9594)

* *brothers*: This was a common way to address each other ([13:15](https://ref.ly/Acts13:15), [26](https://ref.ly/Acts13:26), [38](https://ref.ly/Acts13:38); [Genesis 27:29](https://ref.ly/Gen27:29); [Leviticus 10:6](https://ref.ly/Lev10:6); [25:46](https://ref.ly/Lev25:46); [Numbers 20:3](https://ref.ly/Num20:3); [Deuteronomy 1:28](https://ref.ly/Deut1:28); [24:7](https://ref.ly/Deut24:7); [Joshua 22:3–4](https://ref.ly/Josh22:3-Josh22:4); [2 Samuel 2:26](https://ref.ly/2Sam2:26); [Ezekiel 11:15](https://ref.ly/Ezek11:15)).
* *in ignorance*: See [Acts 17:30](https://ref.ly/Acts17:30); [1 Timothy 1:12–14](https://ref.ly/1Tim1:12-1Tim1:14). The Christian message challenges this lack of knowledge. The gospel calls everyone to respond with faith and to repent ([Acts 2:38](https://ref.ly/Acts2:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 3:17

## Acts 3:19 (id: 9596)

Other sections of the book of Acts and the Gospel of Luke explain the good news of "forgiveness" in more detail (see [Acts 5:30–32](https://ref.ly/Acts5:30-Acts5:32); [10:36–43](https://ref.ly/Acts10:36-Acts10:43); [13:26–38](https://ref.ly/Acts13:26-Acts13:38); [Luke 24:25–27](https://ref.ly/Luke24:25-Luke24:27), [45–47](https://ref.ly/Luke24:45-Luke24:47)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 3:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)
## Acts 3:19–21 (id: 9595)

The message of the good news about Jesus (the gospel) brings comfort (see [Matthew 11:28–29](https://ref.ly/Matt11:28-Matt11:29)). The second coming of Jesus will bring *times of refreshing* from God. He will *send Jesus* to his people again.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 3:19-21

## Acts 3:22 (id: 9597)

Jesus fulfills the ancient promise that God would raise up a prophet like Moses (see [Deuteronomy 18:15](https://ref.ly/Deut18:15), [18](https://ref.ly/Deut18:18); compare [John 6:14](https://ref.ly/John6:14); [7:40](https://ref.ly/John7:40)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 3:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Acts 3:23 (id: 9598)

Refusing to obey Jesus, who is God's ultimate prophet, will lead to severe consequences ([John 3:16](https://ref.ly/John3:16), [17](https://ref.ly/John3:17), [36](https://ref.ly/John3:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 3:23

## Acts 3:25 (id: 9599)

*all the families of the earth will be blessed*: God always intended the covenant people to share his blessings with the world, not for the Jews to be selfish and keep God's blessing only for themselves.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 3:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Acts 4:1 (id: 9605)

*Sadducees*: See “The Sadducees” Profile.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 4:1
* **Associated Articles:** The Sadducees (ID: `500538@TyndaleStudyNotesProfiles`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sadducees (ID: `group:Sadducee`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)
## Acts 4:1–22 (id: 9601)

Throughout the Bible, God's people often experienced persecution. God's servants often encountered hostility and opposition ([Deuteronomy 30:7](https://ref.ly/Deut30:7); [1 Kings 18:13](https://ref.ly/1Kgs18:13); [Nehemiah 4:1–3](https://ref.ly/Neh4:1-Neh4:3); [Jeremiah 37–38](https://ref.ly/Jer37:1-Jer38:28); [Matthew 23:34–37](https://ref.ly/Matt23:34-Matt23:37); [Luke 11:49–51](https://ref.ly/Luke11:49-Luke11:51); [1 Thessalonians 2:14–15](https://ref.ly/1Thess2:14-1Thess2:15)). Jesus was also persecuted ([Luke 4:29](https://ref.ly/Luke4:29); [John 5:16](https://ref.ly/John5:16)). He warned his disciples to expect similar treatment ([Matthew 10:23](https://ref.ly/Matt10:23); [24:9](https://ref.ly/Matt24:9); [Mark 13:9](https://ref.ly/Mark13:9); [Luke 21:12](https://ref.ly/Luke21:12); [John 16:2](https://ref.ly/John16:2)). However, he promised that the Holy Spirit would provide them with strength ([Acts 1:8](https://ref.ly/Acts1:8); [Luke 12:11–12](https://ref.ly/Luke12:11-Luke12:12); [21:15](https://ref.ly/Luke21:15)). 

The book of Acts records many examples of persecution ([Acts 4:3](https://ref.ly/Acts4:3); [5:17–41](https://ref.ly/Acts5:17-Acts5:41); [7:54–8:3](https://ref.ly/Acts7:54-Acts8:3); [9:1–2](https://ref.ly/Acts9:1-Acts9:2); [11:19](https://ref.ly/Acts11:19); [12:2](https://ref.ly/Acts12:2); [13:50](https://ref.ly/Acts13:50); [14:19](https://ref.ly/Acts14:19); [16:19–24](https://ref.ly/Acts16:19-Acts16:24)). But it also emphasizes that the Holy Spirit empowers apostles to testify in such situations ([4:8–13](https://ref.ly/Acts4:8-Acts4:13); [6:10](https://ref.ly/Acts6:10); [7:55](https://ref.ly/Acts7:55)). The courage of Peter and John before the hostile Jewish high council shows how Christians should face persecution with courage and power ([4:20](https://ref.ly/Acts4:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 4:1-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Sadducees (ID: `group:Sadducee`); Caiaphas (ID: `person:Caiaphas`); Alexander (ID: `person:Alexander.2`); John (ID: `person:John.3`); Annas (ID: `person:Annas`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Nazareth (ID: `place:Nazareth`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Discern (ID: `keyterm:Discern`); Cornerstone (ID: `realia:Cornerstone`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

*and the number of men grew to about five thousand*: See study note on Matthew 14:21.

* **Associated Articles:** Matthew 14:21 (ID: `21771@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Acts 4:13 (id: 9602)

*they were unschooled, ordinary men*: This means they were not scholars or trained teachers of the Scriptures. Peter and John were working men without higher education. 

The members of the Jewish high council were educated, and people saw them as authorities on the Scriptures and religious matters. Because of this, the council members were amazed to hear untrained men speak so boldly about these topics.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 4:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`)
## Acts 4:16–18 (id: 9603)

They acknowledged the miracle but still tried to prevent the message. Jesus had experienced similar resistance ([Matthew 19:8](https://ref.ly/Matt19:8); [Mark 10:5](https://ref.ly/Mark10:5); see also [Deuteronomy 10:16](https://ref.ly/Deut10:16); [30:6](https://ref.ly/Deut30:6); [Jeremiah 4:4](https://ref.ly/Jer4:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 4:16-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)
## Acts 4:19–20 (id: 9604)

The apostles shared a principle that can guide God’s people in dealing with hostile authorities (see also [5:40](https://ref.ly/Acts5:40); compare [1 Samuel 15:22](https://ref.ly/1Sam15:22); [Jeremiah 7:23](https://ref.ly/Jer7:23); [Luke 20:20–26](https://ref.ly/Luke20:20-Luke20:26); [Romans 13:1–7](https://ref.ly/Rom13:1-Rom13:7)). Jesus had predicted that his disciples would encounter hostility and persecution ([Luke 21:12–19](https://ref.ly/Luke21:12-Luke21:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 4:19-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`)
## Acts 4:23–31 (id: 9606)

The believers heard the report that their leaders had been threatened and commanded never again to speak about Jesus ([4:18](https://ref.ly/Acts4:18)). The Christian movement was clearly under attack from the religious authorities, so they united in prayer. They turned to God, sharing their troubles and asking for courage to speak the message. They also asked God to send signs and wonders to confirm that the message was true. God answered their request in a clear and powerful way.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 4:23-31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Pilate (ID: `person:Pilate`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Portent (ID: `keyterm:Portent`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Empty (ID: `keyterm:Empty`); David (ID: `person:David`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`)
## Acts 4:25–26 (id: 9607)

This passage quotes [Psalm 2:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:2), which describes how the Gentile (non\-Jewish) nations react to the Lord’s anointed king. Similarly, the Jews and Gentiles in Jerusalem had gathered together against Jesus and now against his followers.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 4:25-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Empty (ID: `keyterm:Empty`); David (ID: `person:David`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`)
## Acts 4:26 (id: 9608)

The Hebrew term *Messiah* (which means "anointed one") originally referred to Israel’s anointed king. Later, it referred to the Messiah, God's chosen future king of Israel who would establish God's kingdom forever ([2 Samuel 7:14–16](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:14-2Sam7:16); [Psalm 89:36–37](https://ref.ly/Ps89:36-Ps89:37); [Isaiah 9:6–7](https://ref.ly/Isa9:6-Isa9:7); [11:1–9](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1-Isa11:9); [61:1](https://ref.ly/Isa61:1); [Jeremiah 23:5–6](https://ref.ly/Jer23:5-Jer23:6); [33:14–16](https://ref.ly/Jer33:14-Jer33:16); [Luke 1:32–33](https://ref.ly/Luke1:32-Luke1:33)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 4:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Acts 4:28 (id: 9609)

God had been guiding events to fulfill his purpose. The Old Testament also shows God's guidance in his people's lives (see [Ezra 7:6](https://ref.ly/Ezra7:6), [9](https://ref.ly/Ezra7:9), [28](https://ref.ly/Ezra7:28); [8:18](https://ref.ly/Ezra8:18), [31](https://ref.ly/Ezra8:31); [Nehemiah 2:8](https://ref.ly/Neh2:8), [18](https://ref.ly/Neh2:18); [Ecclesiastes 9:1](https://ref.ly/Eccl9:1); [Ezekiel 8:1](https://ref.ly/Ezek8:1); [Romans 8:28](https://ref.ly/Rom8:28); [1 Peter 5:6](https://ref.ly/1Pet5:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 4:28

## Acts 4:32–35 (id: 9610)

The sharing among the early believers was remarkable (see also [2:44–45](https://ref.ly/Acts2:44-Acts2:45); compare [6:1](https://ref.ly/Acts6:1)). They were united in heart and mind (they thought alike and shared the same purpose). They freely shared what they owned to meet the needs of other believers. This sharing was voluntary and not forced. It responded to real needs in the community and was motivated by Christian love and care for one another.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 4:32-35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); In Common (ID: `keyterm:InCommon`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`); House (ID: `realia:House`)
## Acts 4:36–37 (id: 9611)

Barnabas is presented as an example of generous and unselfish giving in response to real needs in the Christian community.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 4:36-37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Cypriots (ID: `group:Cyprus`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Cyprus (ID: `place:Cyprus`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`)
## Acts 5:1–11 (id: 9613)

Luke likes to balance a positive example with a negative one. He just shared the remarkable and positive story of Barnabas ([4:36–37](https://ref.ly/Acts4:36-Acts4:37)). Now, Luke shows the opposite behavior of Ananias and Sapphira. This couple desired to earn status in the community and for others to admire them. Ananias did not have to sell his property or give away the money. Their sin was pretending and dishonesty.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 5:1-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ananias (ID: `person:Ananias`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Sapphira (ID: `person:Sapphira`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`); Door (ID: `realia:Door`)
## Acts 5:3–4 (id: 9620)

By lying about the sale of their land, Ananias was not just being dishonest towards others but also toward the Holy Spirit. This means he was lying to God. His actions harmed the honesty, unity, and integrity of the church at its foundation. As a result, he experienced God's direct judgment (compare [Leviticus 10:1–5](https://ref.ly/Lev10:1-Lev10:5); [Joshua 7:16–26](https://ref.ly/Josh7:16-Josh7:26)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 5:3-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Ananias (ID: `person:Ananias`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`)
## Acts 5:11 (id: 9614)

*great fear came over the whole church and all who heard about these events*: The sudden judgment on Ananias and Sapphira had a serious impact on both the Christian community and those who learned about it (see [Hebrews 10:31](https://ref.ly/Heb10:31)). Christians were strongly reminded to remain pure. 

For non\-Christians, the integrity of Christians was restored. So once again "the people regarded them highly" ([Acts 5:13](https://ref.ly/Acts5:13)). However, it also made those outside the church cautious about joining a group with such high standards ([5:13](https://ref.ly/Acts5:13)). This divine "pruning" allowed for new growth to occur (see [John 15:1–11](https://ref.ly/John15:1-John15:11); [Acts 5:14](https://ref.ly/Acts5:14); see also [6:5–7](https://ref.ly/Acts6:5-Acts6:7); [11:24](https://ref.ly/Acts11:24); [21:20](https://ref.ly/Acts21:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 5:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`)
## Acts 5:12–16 (id: 9615)

The preaching of the apostles included many miraculous signs and wonders, similar to the ministry of Jesus. There were also powerful acts of healing and casting out demons (see also [6:8](https://ref.ly/Acts6:8); compare [Matthew 4:24](https://ref.ly/Matt4:24); [9:35](https://ref.ly/Matt9:35); [Mark 1:32–34](https://ref.ly/Mark1:32-Mark1:34); [Luke 4:40–41](https://ref.ly/Luke4:40-Luke4:41)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 5:12-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Portent (ID: `keyterm:Portent`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Porch (ID: `realia:Porch`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)
## Acts 5:13 (id: 9616)

* *Although the people regarded them highly*: People respected the believers because their way of life was different.
* *no one else dared to join them*: The people may have been afraid because of the high level of commitment expected of believers. They may also have feared what happened to Ananias and Sapphira ([5:11](https://ref.ly/Acts5:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 5:13

## Acts 5:17–40 (id: 9617)

The apostles' successful ministry caused strong opposition (compare [4:1–3](https://ref.ly/Acts4:1-Acts4:3)). The wealthy and powerful Sadducees controlled the temple and had a temple police force. They opposed belief in the resurrection and were determined to challenge and, if possible, prevent the apostles from sharing their message about the resurrection of Jesus. They felt their influence over the Jewish people weakening. Jealousy and bitterness led them to attack the apostles.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 5:17-40

## Acts 5:19–20 (id: 9618)

The irony is that the Sadducees denied the existence of angels ([23:8](https://ref.ly/Acts23:8)). However, angels appear many times in the book of Acts (see also [10:3](https://ref.ly/Acts10:3), [7](https://ref.ly/Acts10:7), [22](https://ref.ly/Acts10:22); [11:13](https://ref.ly/Acts11:13); [12:6–11](https://ref.ly/Acts12:6-Acts12:11), [23](https://ref.ly/Acts12:23); [27:23–24](https://ref.ly/Acts27:23-Acts27:24)). Luke shows God guiding his people in their ministry, using angels to carry out his purpose.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 5:19-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); Door (ID: `realia:Door`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Acts 5:34 (id: 9623)

Gamaliel was a respected Pharisee and a leading member of the Jewish high council. He was also a well\-known teacher of the law. He later taught Paul during his training to become a Jewish rabbi (see [22:3](https://ref.ly/Acts22:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 5:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gamaliel (ID: `person:Gamaliel`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`)
## Acts 5:36–37 (id: 9624)

* This Theudas is otherwise unknown, although the Jewish historian Josephus mentions a different man with the same name who led a revolution some years later (see Josephus, *Antiquities* 20\.5\.1\). Theudas was a common Jewish name.
* Judas of Galilee appears in Josephus as a rebel who opposed paying taxes to Caesar around AD 6 or 7, following the census of Quirinius (see Josephus, *Antiquities* 20\.5\.2; compare [Luke 2:2](https://ref.ly/Luke2:2)). His resistance was stopped by the Roman authorities, who used force to end the revolt

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 5:36-37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Judas (ID: `person:Judas.3`); Theudas (ID: `person:Theudas`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Census (ID: `keyterm:Census`); Galileans (ID: `group:Galilee`)
## Acts 6:1 (id: 9626)

* *the Grecian Jews*: The Greek\-speaking Christians were Jewish Christians from other parts of the Greek and Roman world. They likely followed some Greek customs. The Hebrew\-speaking Christians were more traditional Jews from Palestine. They spoke Hebrew or Aramaic and avoided Greek customs.
* *their widows were being overlooked*: This injustice threatened the church's growth and development. Overlooking needs from one group caused frustration and required an intentional response to correct the behavior.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 6:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hellenists (ID: `group:Hellenist`); Greece (ID: `place:Greece`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Hebrews (ID: `group:Hebrews`)
## Acts 6:2–6 (id: 9627)

* The apostles showed wisdom by calling a meeting of all the Christians to resolve the dispute. They understood that the problem was very serious. They also knew their priority was teaching God's word. They decided to choose men who were wise and filled with the Holy Spirit to manage the food distribution.

    The community agreed with the plan the apostles proposed. They selected capable leaders from the Christians who spoke Greek (all of their names are Greek). These seven men focused on this special task, allowing the apostles to pray and teach.

* These seven men are sometimes called the first deacons because their role is similar to that of the office of deacon (see [1 Timothy 3:8–13](https://ref.ly/1Tim3:8-1Tim3:13)). Although the Greek term translated “deacon” (Greek *diakonos*, “servant”) is not used here, a related Greek word is translated "to wait on tables" ([6:2](https://ref.ly/Acts6:2); Greek *diakonein*, “to serve”).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 6:2-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Philip (ID: `person:Philip.4`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Nicanor (ID: `person:Nicanor`); Prochorus (ID: `person:Prochorus`); Parmenas (ID: `person:Parmenas`); Timon (ID: `person:Timon`); Stephen (ID: `person:Stephen`); Antiochians (ID: `group:Antioch`); Nicolaus (ID: `person:Nicolaus`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`); Antioch (ID: `place:Antioch`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Acts 6:7 (id: 9628)

* The apostles were wise in how they dealt with the issue. This led to an increase in the number of Christians, including many of the Jewish priests.
* his is the first of three places in Acts where Luke notes that God’s message continued to spread (see also [12:24](https://ref.ly/Acts12:24); [19:20](https://ref.ly/Acts19:20)). When serious difficulties arose, God’s message overcame internal conflict, idolatry, and pagan practices. As a result, the community continued to grow. The book of Acts tells the story of how the good news about Jesus spread without being stopped (see [28:31](https://ref.ly/Acts28:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 6:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Acts 6:8–15 (id: 9629)

The Jewish leaders did to Stephen as they had done to Jesus (compare [Matthew 26:3–4](https://ref.ly/Matt26:3-Matt26:4), [59–66](https://ref.ly/Matt26:59-Matt26:66)):

* They found someone to lie about him,
* they accused him of blasphemy (saying offensive things about God)*,*
* they started a riot,
* they arrested him, and
* they made false charges against him.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 6:8-15

## Acts 6:15 (id: 9625)

*his face was like the face of an angel*: This is similar to when the face of Moses "was radiant" after speaking with the Lord ([6:14](https://ref.ly/Acts6:14); see [Exodus 34:32–35](https://ref.ly/Exod34:32-Exod34:35)). Stephen, like Moses, was bringing God’s instruction to Israel (see also [2 Corinthians 3:7–18](https://ref.ly/2Cor3:7-2Cor3:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 6:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)
## Acts 7:1–53 (id: 9630)

* Stephen answered the accusations by speaking about his Lord (see [Luke 21:12–17](https://ref.ly/Luke21:12-Luke21:17)). Instead of defending himself, he became a witness in God's legal case against them. This revealed how contrary they were and their lack of loyalty. Stephen gave a history of Israel's past and reminded them of their repeated rejections of those who announced God's messages.
* Stephen's review of Israel's history has three main parts:

    1. The work of the patriarchs. This refers to Abraham and those descended from him who founded the nation of Israel ([Acts 7:2–16](https://ref.ly/Acts7:2-Acts7:16)).
        2. The ministry of Moses ([7:17–43](https://ref.ly/Acts7:17-Acts7:43)).
        3. The role of the tabernacle and the temple ([7:44–50](https://ref.ly/Acts7:44-Acts7:50)).

    Stephen ended his historical survey with a strong criticism of his people's lack of obedience. Like a prophet, he challenged them to stop resisting the Holy Spirit. He told them to turn to God with grief for their sins (repentance) and faith.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 7:1-53

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); To Act Wickedly (ID: `keyterm:ActWickedly`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Rescue (ID: `keyterm:Rescue`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Promise (ID: `keyterm:Promise`); Judge (ID: `keyterm:Judge`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Mount Sinai (ID: `place:SinaiMount`); Haran (ID: `place:Haran`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Rephan (ID: `deity:Rephan`); Shechem (ID: `place:Shechem`); Hamor (ID: `person:Hamor`); Mesopotamia (ID: `place:Mesopotamia`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Circumcision (ID: `keyterm:Circumcision`); Bow Down (ID: `keyterm:BowDown`); Joshua (ID: `person:Joshua.2`)

## Acts 7:14 (id: 9631)

*seventy\-five*: Compare this number with the seventy mentioned in the Hebrew text of [Genesis 46:27](https://ref.ly/Gen46:27). The number seventy\-five probably comes from the Septuagint, the ancient Greek translation of the Old Testament. The Septuagint includes three people descended from Ephraim and two of Manasseh in [46:20](https://ref.ly/Gen46:20). This adds five more people. The Hebrew text does not include those descended from Joseph’s sons. New Testament authors mostly quote from the Septuagint, which sometimes differs from the Hebrew Masoretic Text.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 7:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)
## Acts 7:16 (id: 9632)

*a price*: This was 400 pieces of silver, according to [Genesis 23:16](https://ref.ly/Gen23:16).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 7:16

## Acts 7:37 (id: 9633)

*a prophet like me:* This verse quotes [Deuteronomy 18:15](https://ref.ly/Deut18:15). Moses prophesied that the Messiah would come. It happened as Moses said.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 7:37

## Acts 7:39–40 (id: 9634)

The ancient Jews rejected the law Moses gave to them for their salvation. Stephen was speaking to the people of his time who were physical and spiritual descendants from those Jews. His point was that they were still rejecting God's messengers. In this case, it was Jesus, the ultimate savior.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 7:39-40

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`)
## Acts 7:42–43 (id: 9635)

This passage quotes [Amos 5:25–27](https://ref.ly/Amos5:25-Amos5:27) (Greek version) to show how the people of Israel rebelled against Moses ([Acts 7:39–41](https://ref.ly/Acts7:39-Acts7:41)). 

Molech was a Canaanite god to whom human sacrifices were offered ([Leviticus 18:21](https://ref.ly/Lev18:21); [20:2–5](https://ref.ly/Lev20:2-Lev20:5); [1 Kings 11:7](https://ref.ly/1Kgs11:7); [2 Kings 23:10](https://ref.ly/2Kgs23:10); [Jeremiah 32:35](https://ref.ly/Jer32:35)). 

Rephan*,* a star god identified with the planet Saturn, was worshiped by the Israelites during their wilderness wanderings.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 7:42-43

## Acts 7:48–50 (id: 9636)

One charge against Stephen was that he spoke against the temple (see [6:14](https://ref.ly/Acts6:14)). The leading priests and scribes managed the temple's business and wanted to protect their interests (see [Luke 19:45–48](https://ref.ly/Luke19:45-Luke19:48)). This is why these leaders were concerned about the temple, even though God said that the Most High does not live in temples made by human hands.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 7:48-50

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Throne (ID: `realia:Throne`); Footstool (ID: `realia:Footstool`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)
## Acts 7:51 (id: 9637)

Stephen accused the people of being stiff\-necked with uncircumcised hearts and ears.This means they were strong\-willed and ignored God's truth. This was exactly how God had accused them of behaving during the time of wilderness wandering. Here, Stephen links it to rejecting the gospel and resisting the Holy Spirit (see [Exodus 32:9](https://ref.ly/Exod32:9); [33:3](https://ref.ly/Exod33:3); [34:9](https://ref.ly/Exod34:9); [Deuteronomy 9:6](https://ref.ly/Deut9:6), [13](https://ref.ly/Deut9:13); [31:27](https://ref.ly/Deut31:27); see also [Psalm 78:8](https://ref.ly/Ps78:8); [Zechariah 7:11–12](https://ref.ly/Zech7:11-Zech7:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 7:51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)
## Acts 7:55–56 (id: 9638)

* *standing at the right hand of God*: Usually, the Bible describes Jesus as seated in heaven at God’s right side ([2:33–34](https://ref.ly/Acts2:33-Acts2:34); [5:31](https://ref.ly/Acts5:31); [Luke 20:42](https://ref.ly/Luke20:42); [22:69](https://ref.ly/Luke22:69); [Romans 8:34](https://ref.ly/Rom8:34); [Ephesians 1:20](https://ref.ly/Eph1:20); [Colossians 3:1](https://ref.ly/Col3:1); [Hebrews 1:3](https://ref.ly/Heb1:3), [13](https://ref.ly/Heb1:13); [10:12](https://ref.ly/Heb10:12)). One possible explanation has been proposed by some church fathers that Jesus was welcoming Stephen to heaven with honor as the first New Testament martyr. Stephen was loyal in confessing his Lord on earth. Now his Lord honored his promise to confess his loyal servant in heaven. Jesus was standing as a witness to defend him ([Matthew 10:32](https://ref.ly/Matt10:32); [Luke 12:8](https://ref.ly/Luke12:8)).
* *Son of Man standing*: Compare [Daniel 7:13–14](https://ref.ly/Dan7:13-Dan7:14). The Jewish leaders understood that Stephen was speaking of Jesus as the divine Son of Man ([Acts 7:57](https://ref.ly/Acts7:57)). This title speaks of Jesus’s power and authority (compare [Revelation 1:12–15](https://ref.ly/Rev1:12-Rev1:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 7:55-56

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`)
## Acts 7:57 (id: 9639)

They covered their ears because they thought comparing Jesus to the divine Son of Man was a terrible offense against God ([7:56](https://ref.ly/Acts7:56)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 7:57

## Acts 7:58 (id: 9640)

*Saul* was his name in Hebrew, and *Paul* was his Greek name (see [13:9](https://ref.ly/Acts13:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 7:58

## Acts 7:59–60 (id: 9641)

Stephen’s prayer, "Lord, do not hold this sin against them," is very similar to the prayer Jesus prayed when he was on the cross ([Luke 23:34](https://ref.ly/Luke23:34)). Jesus taught his followers the importance of forgiveness and prayer ([Matthew 6:14–15](https://ref.ly/Matt6:14-Matt6:15); [Mark 11:25](https://ref.ly/Mark11:25); see [Luke 11:4](https://ref.ly/Luke11:4); [17:3–4](https://ref.ly/Luke17:3-Luke17:4); [Luke 11:5–10](https://ref.ly/Luke11:5-Luke11:10); [18:1–8](https://ref.ly/Luke18:1-Luke18:8); see also [Acts 1:12–15](https://ref.ly/Acts1:12-Acts1:15); [4:23–31](https://ref.ly/Acts4:23-Acts4:31); [12:5](https://ref.ly/Acts12:5); [James 5:16–18](https://ref.ly/Jas5:16-Jas5:18)). The Lord gave a positive answer to Stephen’s prayer as it relates to Saul ([Acts 9:1–43](https://ref.ly/Acts9:1-Acts9:43)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 7:59-60

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Call Upon (ID: `keyterm:CallUpon`); Stephen (ID: `person:Stephen`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`)
## Acts 8:1–4 (id: 9643)

*Saul was there*: Compare [9:1–2](https://ref.ly/Acts9:1-Acts9:2); [22:4](https://ref.ly/Acts22:4); [26:9–11](https://ref.ly/Acts26:9-Acts26:11); [Galatians 1:13–14](https://ref.ly/Gal1:13-Gal1:14). This major attack against the church caused all believers except the apostles to flee. Instead of losing enthusiasm, they preached the good news about Jesus wherever they went.

## Acts 8:7 (id: 9651)

*Came out … healed*: Miracles often happen when the gospel is preached in the book of Acts (see study note on 3:1–11).

## Acts 8:9–24 (id: 9652)

Simon the sorcerer performed magic and amazed the people of Samaria. He boasted that he was someone great. Simon tried to buy spiritual power. The later corrupt practice of buying and selling positions of leadership in the church was called *simony*. This term refers to the corrupt practice of buying and selling church leadership positions (see [1 Timothy 6:9–10](https://ref.ly/1Tim6:9-1Tim6:10)). Peter strongly criticized Simon's evil intentions and actions.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 8:9-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Simon (ID: `person:Simon.8`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Philip (ID: `person:Philip.4`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Unjust Deed (ID: `keyterm:UnjustDeed`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Simon (ID: `person:Simon.9`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Straightness (ID: `keyterm:Straightness`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)
## Acts 8:14–17 (id: 9644)

When the apostles sent two of their own representatives to Samaria, it was an important step in overcoming prejudice (compare [Matthew 10:5](https://ref.ly/Matt10:5); [Luke 9:52–54](https://ref.ly/Luke9:52-Luke9:54); [John 4:9](https://ref.ly/John4:9); [8:48](https://ref.ly/John8:48)). It was even more remarkable that Peter and John prayed for the Samaritans who trusted in Christ to receive the Holy Spirit. This event is similar to the day of Pentecost in Jerusalem and marks how the Holy Spirit's power spread from Judea to Samaria ([Acts 2:1–47](https://ref.ly/Acts2:1-Acts2:47); [1:8](https://ref.ly/Acts1:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 8:14-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`)
## Acts 8:24 (id: 9645)

Simon recognized that he needed Peter to pray for him. However, the text does not clearly say that Simon repented or turned away from his evil actions. His request seems focused on avoiding the severe punishment Peter warned about, rather than on a true change in how he thought and acted.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 8:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simon (ID: `person:Simon.8`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)
## Acts 8:26–40 (id: 9646)

Philip obeyed the Holy Spirit and followed the Spirit’s direction. He then took the opportunity to share the message of the gospel with an Ethiopian eunuch (a trusted male official who served a king or queen) on the desert road from Jerusalem to Gaza. This event shows how effective Philip was in explaining and sharing the Christian message. It also shows his strong commitment to bringing the good news to all people, no matter their social position or ethnic background.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 8:26-40

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Philip (ID: `person:Philip.4`); Public Official (ID: `person:GenericOfficial`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Isaiah (ID: `person:Isaiah`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Written (ID: `keyterm:Scripture`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ethiopia (ID: `place:Ethiopia`); Ethiopians (ID: `group:Ethiopia`); Gaza (ID: `place:Gaza`); Ashdod (ID: `place:Ashdod`); Bow Down (ID: `keyterm:BowDown`); Caesarea (ID: `place:Caesarea`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)
## Acts 8:27 (id: 9647)

* In the ancient world, a eunuch was a royal official. Some eunuchs had been physically altered and could not have children, while others held the title because of their role in the royal court (see [2 Kings 9:30–32](https://ref.ly/2Kgs9:30-2Kgs9:32); [Esther 1:10](https://ref.ly/Esth1:10); [2:3](https://ref.ly/Esth2:3), [14–15](https://ref.ly/Esth2:14-Esth2:15), [21](https://ref.ly/Esth2:21); [4:4–5](https://ref.ly/Esth4:4-Esth4:5)).

    Many Jews looked down on eunuchs. Eunuchs could not have children, so they could not pass on the covenant family line (God’s people through descendants). The law of Moses also excluded men with damaged genitals from the assembly of Israel, which was the gathered worshiping community ([Deuteronomy 23:1](https://ref.ly/Deut23:1); compare [Leviticus 21:17–23](https://ref.ly/Lev21:17-Lev21:23)).

    However, the prophet Isaiah spoke of a future time when God would welcome non\-Jewish people and eunuchs who were faithful to him ([Isaiah 56:3–8](https://ref.ly/Isa56:3-Isa56:8); see also [Matthew 19:12](https://ref.ly/Matt19:12)). In the new covenant, all people who have genuine faith belong to the people of God.

* The eunuch traveled from Africa to Jerusalem to worship in the temple, probably for a major Jewish feast.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 8:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ethiopia (ID: `place:Ethiopia`); Ethiopians (ID: `group:Ethiopia`); Bow Down (ID: `keyterm:BowDown`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)
## Acts 8:29 (id: 9648)

The Holy Spirit guides the servants of God in where, when, and what to preach, teach, or do ([Acts 9:15](https://ref.ly/Acts9:15); [10:19–20](https://ref.ly/Acts10:19-Acts10:20); [11:12](https://ref.ly/Acts11:12); [16:6](https://ref.ly/Acts16:6); [1 Corinthians 2:13](https://ref.ly/1Cor2:13); [1 Peter 1:12](https://ref.ly/1Pet1:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 8:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Philip (ID: `person:Philip.4`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`)

The Scripture passage was [Isaiah 53:7–8](https://ref.ly/Isa53:7-Isa53:8). It is one of the Servant Songs of Isaiah, a passage that speaks of the suffering servant of the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 8:32-33

## Acts 8:39–40 (id: 9650)

* After the Spirit of the Lord carried Philip away, he traveled north along the coast from Azotus. Azotus is another name for Ashdod (see [1 Samuel 5:1–7](https://ref.ly/1Sam5:1-1Sam5:7); [Nehemiah 13:23–24](https://ref.ly/Neh13:23-Neh13:24); [Isaiah 20:1](https://ref.ly/Isa20:1)). He preached in every town until he reached Caesarea, where he later settled (see [Acts 21:8](https://ref.ly/Acts21:8)).
* *Caesarea:* Caesarea Maritima was a major port on the Mediterranean Sea. It was the center of Roman government in Judea. Herod the Great built it around 22 to 9 BC and named it to honor Caesar Augustus, the Roman emperor.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 8:39-40

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Philip (ID: `person:Philip.4`); Ashdod (ID: `place:Ashdod`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Caesarea (ID: `place:Caesarea`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`)
## Acts 9:1–19 (id: 9653)

The conversion of Saul of Tarsus on the Damascus road is central to the story in Acts. Luke tells the story three times (also [22:1–21](https://ref.ly/Acts22:1-Acts22:21); [26:1–29](https://ref.ly/Acts26:1-Acts26:29)). Paul (Saul) mentions this experience several times in his letters ([1 Corinthians 15:8–10](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:8-1Cor15:10); [Galatians 1:11–17](https://ref.ly/Gal1:11-Gal1:17); [Philippians 3:4–11](https://ref.ly/Phil3:4-Phil3:11); see [1 Timothy 1:12–17](https://ref.ly/1Tim1:12-1Tim1:17)). Saul’s conversion was his call as a prophet and commission as an apostle ([Acts 9:15](https://ref.ly/Acts9:15); [22:15](https://ref.ly/Acts22:15), [21](https://ref.ly/Acts22:21); [26:15–18](https://ref.ly/Acts26:15-Acts26:18)). No one is beyond God's power to reach, save, and use for holy purposes. Nothing is impossible with God ([Luke 1:37](https://ref.ly/Luke1:37)). God prepared Paul through his training, childhood, and experience to play a unique role in proclaiming the gospel to the wider world as the “apostle to the Gentiles” ([Romans 11:13](https://ref.ly/Rom11:13); see [1 Corinthians 15:9](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:9); [2 Corinthians 12:11–12](https://ref.ly/2Cor12:11-2Cor12:12); [Galatians 1:1](https://ref.ly/Gal1:1); [Ephesians 3:8](https://ref.ly/Eph3:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 9:1-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Damascus (ID: `place:Damascus`); Ananias (ID: `person:Ananias.2`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); House (ID: `realia:House`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Straight Street (ID: `place:Straight`); Judas (ID: `person:Judas.4`); Tarsians (ID: `group:Tarsus`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Call Upon (ID: `keyterm:CallUpon`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Tarsus (ID: `place:Tarsus`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Straightness (ID: `keyterm:Straightness`)
## Acts 9:2 (id: 9662)

* A synagogue (Greek *sunagōgē*, meaning “gathering place”) was a local Jewish meeting place. After the exile to Babylon, Jewish people began to meet in synagogues in their towns. These places became centers for teaching and worship. After Israel returned from exile in Babylon, Jews began using synagogues for instruction and worship.

    In the synagogue, people read from the Law and the Prophets. Leaders explained the meaning of the Scriptures. The people also prayed and gave praise and thanks to God (see [13:15](https://ref.ly/Acts13:15); [15:21](https://ref.ly/Acts15:21); [Nehemiah 9:5](https://ref.ly/Neh9:5); [Matthew 6:5](https://ref.ly/Matt6:5); [Luke 4:16–21](https://ref.ly/Luke4:16-Luke4:21)). Jesus attended, taught, preached, and performed miracles in synagogues ([Matthew 12:9–10](https://ref.ly/Matt12:9-Matt12:10); [Mark 1:21](https://ref.ly/Mark1:21), [39](https://ref.ly/Mark1:39); [Luke 4:16](https://ref.ly/Luke4:16); [13:10–13](https://ref.ly/Luke13:10-Luke13:13); [John 6:59](https://ref.ly/John6:59); [18:20](https://ref.ly/John18:20)). His apostles did as well (see [Acts 9:20](https://ref.ly/Acts9:20); [13:5](https://ref.ly/Acts13:5), [14](https://ref.ly/Acts13:14); [14:1](https://ref.ly/Acts14:1); [17:1](https://ref.ly/Acts17:1), [10](https://ref.ly/Acts17:10), [17](https://ref.ly/Acts17:17); [18:4](https://ref.ly/Acts18:4), [19](https://ref.ly/Acts18:19), [26](https://ref.ly/Acts18:26)).

* *Damascus:* Damascus, the capital of Syria, was an important center with a long history. It was the nearest major city outside of Palestine. It took four to six days to travel from Jerusalem to Damascus. This emphasizes Saul’s determination ([9:1–5](https://ref.ly/Acts9:1-Acts9:5); see [22:4–8](https://ref.ly/Acts22:4-Acts22:8); [26:9–15](https://ref.ly/Acts26:9-Acts26:15)).
* *the Way*: The term *the Way* in the book of Acts refers to Christianity (see [19:9](https://ref.ly/Acts19:9), [23](https://ref.ly/Acts19:23); [24:14](https://ref.ly/Acts24:14), [22](https://ref.ly/Acts24:22)). It is “the way of God” that teaches people “the way of salvation” ([18:26](https://ref.ly/Acts18:26); [16:17](https://ref.ly/Acts16:17)). See also [John 14:6](https://ref.ly/John14:6); [2 Peter 2:2](https://ref.ly/2Pet2:2).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 9:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Damascus (ID: `place:Damascus`)
## Acts 9:10 (id: 9654)

* *Ananias*: See also [22:12](https://ref.ly/Acts22:12). Ananias was a common name (consider the husband of Sapphira, [5:1](https://ref.ly/Acts5:1), and the Jewish high priest \[AD 47–59], [23:2](https://ref.ly/Acts23:2)).
* *a vision*: See “Visions” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 9:10
* **Associated Articles:** Visions (ID: `500385@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ananias (ID: `person:Ananias.2`); Damascus (ID: `place:Damascus`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)
## Acts 9:15 (id: 9655)

*This man is My chosen instrument to carry My name before the Gentiles*: In God’s plan to spread the good news, the next step was to reach the Gentiles, that is, people from other nations (see [1:8](https://ref.ly/Acts1:8); compare chapters [10–11](https://ref.ly/Acts10:1-Acts11:30)). 

Saul of Tarsus, also called Paul, was the one God chose for this task. God sent him to bring the message about Jesus to Gentiles, to kings, and also to the people of Israel.

The rest of the book of Acts shows how Paul faithfully carried out this mission from God (for example, [26:19–23](https://ref.ly/Acts26:19-Acts26:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 9:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)
## Acts 9:16 (id: 9656)

As Jesus predicted, Paul suffered greatly for his faith (see [2 Corinthians 11:23–27](https://ref.ly/2Cor11:23-2Cor11:27); see [Acts 13:45](https://ref.ly/Acts13:45); [14:19](https://ref.ly/Acts14:19); [16:22–27](https://ref.ly/Acts16:22-Acts16:27); [21:30–31](https://ref.ly/Acts21:30-Acts21:31); [26:21](https://ref.ly/Acts26:21); [2 Timothy 1:11–12](https://ref.ly/2Tim1:11-2Tim1:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 9:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

God gave Ananias a special role. He was to welcome Saul into the Christian community.

Ananias began by placing his hands on Saul. Through this act, Saul was healed and filled with the Holy Spirit.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 9:17
