## Acts 9:20–21 (id: 9658)

*Saul promptly began to proclaim Jesus*: Saul's encounter with the heavenly Christ was genuine, shown by his enthusiastic and bold preaching. His direct declaration surprised many because he had previously caused great harm to the followers of Jesus in Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 9:20-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Call Upon (ID: `keyterm:CallUpon`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)
## Acts 9:22–25 (id: 9659)

The people who heard Saul were confused and surprised. Even so, Saul clearly showed from the Scriptures that Jesus is the Messiah. His arguments were so strong that the Jews in Damascus who did not believe could not answer him.

This continued for some time (see [Galatians 1:18](https://ref.ly/Gal1:18)). The people who heard Saul were confused and surprised. Even so, Saul clearly showed from the Scriptures that Jesus is the Messiah. His arguments were so strong that the Jews in Damascus who did not believe could not answer him.

This continued for some time in [2 Corinthians 11:32–33](https://ref.ly/2Cor11:32-2Cor11:33).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 9:22-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Damascus (ID: `place:Damascus`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Basket (ID: `realia:Basket`); City Wall (ID: `realia:CityWall`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)
## Acts 9:26–28 (id: 9660)

*When Saul arrived in Jerusalem:* The believers did not trust him at first. This was understandable. He had recently attacked and imprisoned Christians ([8:3](https://ref.ly/Acts8:3); [9:1](https://ref.ly/Acts9:1), [13](https://ref.ly/Acts9:13); [22:3](https://ref.ly/Acts22:3), [4](https://ref.ly/Acts22:4); [26:9–11](https://ref.ly/Acts26:9-Acts26:11)). 

Barnabas helped him. Barnabas, whose name means “Son of Encouragement” ([4:36](https://ref.ly/Acts4:36)), brought Saul to the apostles. He told them how Saul had seen the Lord on the road to Damascus. He also explained how Saul had preached boldly in the name of Jesus there.

Barnabas convinced the apostles that Saul’s conversion was real. So Saul stayed with them in Jerusalem. There, he continued to preach boldly about the Lord.

Paul later spoke about this visit in [Galatians 1:18–19](https://ref.ly/Gal1:18-Gal1:19).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 9:26-28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Damascus (ID: `place:Damascus`)
## Acts 9:29 (id: 9661)

Once again, Saul debated with some Greek\-speaking Jews. He spoke boldly and explained why Jesus is the Messiah.

Again, some people became angry and made a plan to kill him (see [9:22–24](https://ref.ly/Acts9:22-Acts9:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 9:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hellenists (ID: `group:Hellenist`); Greece (ID: `place:Greece`)
## Acts 9:30 (id: 9663)

After the Christians heard about the plot, they sent Saul away again (see [9:25](https://ref.ly/Acts9:25)). This time he went to Tarsus, his hometown, the capital of Cilicia (see [21:39](https://ref.ly/Acts21:39); [22:3](https://ref.ly/Acts22:3); [23:34](https://ref.ly/Acts23:34); compare [Galatians 1:21](https://ref.ly/Gal1:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 9:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tarsus (ID: `place:Tarsus`); Caesarea (ID: `place:Caesarea`)
## Acts 9:31 (id: 9664)

* This verse is one of several in Acts that describe how the church grew in numbers and in faithfulness (see also [2:41](https://ref.ly/Acts2:41); [4:4](https://ref.ly/Acts4:4); [5:14](https://ref.ly/Acts5:14); [6:1](https://ref.ly/Acts6:1), [7](https://ref.ly/Acts6:7); [12:24](https://ref.ly/Acts12:24); [21:20](https://ref.ly/Acts21:20)).
* The first Christians were learning an important truth: “The fear of the LORD is the beginning of wisdom.” This means that true knowledge starts with honoring and respecting God ([Proverbs 1:7](https://ref.ly/Prov1:7); [9:10](https://ref.ly/Prov9:10); compare [Job 28:28](https://ref.ly/Job28:28); [Psalm 111:10](https://ref.ly/Ps111:10); [Ecclesiastes 12:13](https://ref.ly/Eccl12:13); see [Luke 7:16](https://ref.ly/Luke7:16)).

    As they learned to honor God, they grew stronger in their faith. Their reverence for the Lord helped the church grow (compare [Acts 2:43](https://ref.ly/Acts2:43); [19:17](https://ref.ly/Acts19:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 9:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`)
## Acts 9:32–43 (id: 9665)

* These verses describe Peter’s traveling ministry in Judea, especially in towns along the seacoast. As he traveled, God gave him power to do miracles. Peter healed a man named Aeneas and raised a woman named Dorcas from the dead.

    Jesus had promised that his disciples would do such works ([John 14:12](https://ref.ly/John14:12)).

* Luke often tells stories in pairs. For example, he tells of a man being healed and then a woman being healed (see [Luke 13:10–17](https://ref.ly/Luke13:10-Luke13:17); [14:1–6](https://ref.ly/Luke14:1-Luke14:6)). In this passage, Peter heals both a man and a woman.

    The people in that area were deeply affected by these miracles. Many people turned to the Lord and believed ([Acts 9:35](https://ref.ly/Acts9:35), [42](https://ref.ly/Acts9:42)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 9:32-43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Joppa (ID: `place:Joppa`); Tabitha (ID: `person:Tabitha`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Lod (ID: `place:Lod`); Aeneas (ID: `person:Aeneas`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Upstairs Room (ID: `realia:UpstairsRoom`); Sharon Plain (ID: `place:Sharon.2`); Simon (ID: `person:Simon.9`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`); Tunic (ID: `realia:Tunic.2`); Leather (ID: `realia:Leather`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`)
## Acts 9:43 (id: 9666)

*stayed for several days in Joppa with a tanner named Simon*: A tanner worked with animal skins to make leather. This job required contact with dead animals.

Many Jews considered this kind of work unclean. Because Peter stayed in the home of a tanner, it may show that he was not strict about some Jewish traditions (see [Galatians 2:11–14](https://ref.ly/Gal2:11-Gal2:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 9:43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simon (ID: `person:Simon.9`); Joppa (ID: `place:Joppa`); Leather (ID: `realia:Leather`)
## Acts 10:1–8 (id: 9292)

* *a centurion*: This was the highest rank for an officer in the Roman army who was not commissioned. He led a *century,* a group of about 100 men. Luke often describes centurions positively ([10:22](https://ref.ly/Acts10:22); [21:32](https://ref.ly/Acts21:32); [22:25–26](https://ref.ly/Acts22:25-Acts22:26); [23:17](https://ref.ly/Acts23:17), [23](https://ref.ly/Acts23:23); [27:6](https://ref.ly/Acts27:6), [11](https://ref.ly/Acts27:11), [43](https://ref.ly/Acts27:43); [28:16](https://ref.ly/Acts28:16); [Luke 7:1–10](https://ref.ly/Luke7:1-Luke7:10); [23:47](https://ref.ly/Luke23:47)).

    Luke wanted to show that Christianity was not against Roman officials or institutions. It could, like Judaism, be permitted in the Roman Empire (see Acts Book Introduction, “Purposes of Acts: Politics”).

* *in what was called the Italian Regiment*: A regiment included six centuries; a Roman legion was usually divided into ten regiments. The New Testament mentions the Italian Regiment and the Imperial Regiment ([Acts 27:1](https://ref.ly/Acts27:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 10:1-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Cornelius (ID: `person:Cornelius`); Joppa (ID: `place:Joppa`); Italians (ID: `group:Italy`); Simon (ID: `person:Simon.9`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Italy (ID: `place:Italy`); Caesarea (ID: `place:Caesarea`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Leather (ID: `realia:Leather`); House (ID: `realia:House`)
## Acts 10:2 (id: 9296)

*devout and God\-fearing*: Gentiles described as God\-fearing admired Judaism's high ethical standards. However, they were not ready to undergo circumcision or fully convert to Judaism (see [13:43](https://ref.ly/Acts13:43)). 

Christianity appealed to these Gentiles who worshiped God ([18:7](https://ref.ly/Acts18:7); see [13:48](https://ref.ly/Acts13:48); [16:30](https://ref.ly/Acts16:30); [17:4](https://ref.ly/Acts17:4), [12](https://ref.ly/Acts17:12), [17](https://ref.ly/Acts17:17)). These individuals were open to the message of the gospel that announced that “there is peace with God through Jesus Christ” ([10:36](https://ref.ly/Acts10:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 10:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)
## Acts 10:3 (id: 9299)

In Acts, visions often happen while people are praying ([9:3–6](https://ref.ly/Acts9:3-Acts9:6), [10–16](https://ref.ly/Acts9:10-Acts9:16); [10:2–6](https://ref.ly/Acts10:2-Acts10:6), [9–12](https://ref.ly/Acts10:9-Acts10:12); [11:5–9](https://ref.ly/Acts11:5-Acts11:9); [12:9–17](https://ref.ly/Acts12:9-Acts12:17); [18:9–10](https://ref.ly/Acts18:9-Acts18:10); [22:17–21](https://ref.ly/Acts22:17-Acts22:21); [23:11](https://ref.ly/Acts23:11)). These visions are not random. They show God's saving work and give divine guidance and encouragement.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 10:3

*Peter was puzzling over*: The events that followed would clarify the meaning of the vision ([10:17–48](https://ref.ly/Acts10:17-Acts10:48); see [11:1–18](https://ref.ly/Acts11:1-Acts11:18)). ## Acts 10:25–26 (id: 9295)

*Cornelius … fell at his feet to worship him*: This act was probably more than just bowing to an important person of rank. The Bible says worship is for God alone (see [14:11–17](https://ref.ly/Acts14:11-Acts14:17); [Exodus 20:3](https://ref.ly/Exod20:3); [Deuteronomy 5:7](https://ref.ly/Deut5:7); [Matthew 22:37–38](https://ref.ly/Matt22:37-Matt22:38); [Mark 12:29–30](https://ref.ly/Mark12:29-Mark12:30); [Luke 10:27](https://ref.ly/Luke10:27); [1 Corinthians 10:14](https://ref.ly/1Cor10:14); [Colossians 3:5](https://ref.ly/Col3:5); [1 Peter 4:3](https://ref.ly/1Pet4:3); [1 John 5:21](https://ref.ly/1John5:21); [Revelation 4:10](https://ref.ly/Rev4:10); [9:20](https://ref.ly/Rev9:20); [22:8–9](https://ref.ly/Rev22:8-Rev22:9)). 

Peter was just a fellow human being whom Cornelius should not worship.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 10:25-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Bow Down (ID: `keyterm:BowDown`); Cornelius (ID: `person:Cornelius`)
## Acts 10:34–35 (id: 9297)

* *God does not show favoritism*: See [Deuteronomy 10:17](https://ref.ly/Deut10:17); [2 Chronicles 19:7](https://ref.ly/2Chr19:7); [Job 34:19](https://ref.ly/Job34:19); [Luke 20:21](https://ref.ly/Luke20:21); [Romans 2:11](https://ref.ly/Rom2:11); [Galatians 2:6](https://ref.ly/Gal2:6); [Colossians 3:25](https://ref.ly/Col3:25); [1 Peter 1:17](https://ref.ly/1Pet1:17).

    The application of this principle is the meaning of Peter’s vision ([10:9–16](https://ref.ly/Acts10:9-Acts10:16)).

* *welcomes those from every nation who fear Him and do what is right*: See [Romans 10:11–13](https://ref.ly/Rom10:11-Rom10:13).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 10:34-35

## Acts 10:36–43 (id: 9298)

Peter often emphasizes the role of the apostles as witnesses to the good news (the message about Jesus’s life, death, and resurrection). He reminds his listeners that they had direct contact with Jesus.

The apostles ate and drank with Jesus after he rose from the dead (see [Luke 24:41–43](https://ref.ly/Luke24:41-Luke24:43)). They saw him alive again. Because they were eyewitnesses of his resurrection, they could testify that Jesus had defeated death (see [Acts 3:15](https://ref.ly/Acts3:15); [4:33](https://ref.ly/Acts4:33); [13:30–31](https://ref.ly/Acts13:30-Acts13:31)). 

Jesus chose the original apostles ahead of time to be his witnesses (see [1:12–26](https://ref.ly/Acts1:12-Acts1:26)). Later, others continued this work. For example, Paul and Barnabas preached and taught in the name of Jesus Christ (see [9:15](https://ref.ly/Acts9:15); [14:1–3](https://ref.ly/Acts14:1-Acts14:3); [26:16](https://ref.ly/Acts26:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 10:36-43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Witness (ID: `keyterm:Witness`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`); Declare (ID: `keyterm:Declare`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Nazareth (ID: `place:Nazareth`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); John (ID: `person:John`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)
## Acts 10:43 (id: 9300)

* Based on what they saw and heard, the apostles could declare that Jesus of Nazareth was the one about whom all the prophets spoke ([10:39–42](https://ref.ly/Acts10:39-Acts10:42)). The whole message and purpose of Scripture center on Christ (see [Luke 24:25–27](https://ref.ly/Luke24:25-Luke24:27), [44–47](https://ref.ly/Luke24:44-Luke24:47); [John 5:39](https://ref.ly/John5:39)).
* The main point of their message was that everyone who believes in Jesus will have sins forgiven through his name (see [Luke 24:47](https://ref.ly/Luke24:47)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 10:43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)
## Acts 10:44–48 (id: 9301)

On the day of Pentecost, Peter told the people that if they stopped sinning, repented, asked forgiveness from God, and received baptism in the name of Jesus Christ, they would "receive the gift of the Holy Spirit" ([2:38](https://ref.ly/Acts2:38); see also [19:1–7](https://ref.ly/Acts19:1-Acts19:7)). 

As Cornelius and his household listened to Peter's message, they received the Holy Spirit just like the Jews had earlier. This event confirmed again that God did not favor one group over another ([10:34–35](https://ref.ly/Acts10:34-Acts10:35)). This event is similar to the day of Pentecost in Jerusalem ([2:1–47](https://ref.ly/Acts2:1-Acts2:47)). It shows that the power of the Holy Spirit now came to the Gentiles as well ([1:8](https://ref.ly/Acts1:8); [2:39](https://ref.ly/Acts2:39)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 10:44-48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Circumcision (ID: `keyterm:Circumcision`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Faithfulness (ID: `keyterm:Faithfulness`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`)
## Acts 10:48 (id: 9302)

Cornelius asked Peter to stay for a few days with them, possibly because he needed more instruction in the Christian faith.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 10:48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)
## Acts 11:1–18 (id: 9304)

Jews traditionally kept themselves separate and did not eat or interact in a social setting with Gentiles ([10:28](https://ref.ly/Acts10:28); [22:21–22](https://ref.ly/Acts22:21-Acts22:22); see [John 4:9](https://ref.ly/John4:9), [27](https://ref.ly/John4:27); [18:28](https://ref.ly/John18:28); [Galatians 2:12–14](https://ref.ly/Gal2:12-Gal2:14)). The Jewish Christians in Jerusalem learned that Gentiles had received the word of God. Then they criticized Peter's unusual actions and asked for an explanation, which Peter provided.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:1-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Joppa (ID: `place:Joppa`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Discern (ID: `keyterm:Discern`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Circumcision (ID: `keyterm:Circumcision`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Caesarea (ID: `place:Caesarea`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); John (ID: `person:John`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Linen Cloth (ID: `realia:LinenCloth`); Chameleon (ID: `fauna:Chameleon`); Lizard (ID: `fauna:Lizard`); Monitor (ID: `fauna:Iguana`); Creeping Animal (ID: `fauna:CreepingAnimal`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`)
## Acts 11:4–17 (id: 9315)

Peter reviewed what had happened. He explained that God had directed the whole series of events ([11:12](https://ref.ly/Acts11:12)). Peter ate with Gentiles (non\-Jews) because God made it evident that he should ([11:4–12](https://ref.ly/Acts11:4-Acts11:12)). Peter then saw the Holy Spirit come upon the Gentiles, realizing God accepted and blessed them like Jews who trusted in Jesus ([11:15–17](https://ref.ly/Acts11:15-Acts11:17); see [1:5](https://ref.ly/Acts1:5)). Peter followed God's will by admitting Gentiles to the church.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:4-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Joppa (ID: `place:Joppa`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Caesarea (ID: `place:Caesarea`); John (ID: `person:John`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Discern (ID: `keyterm:Discern`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Linen Cloth (ID: `realia:LinenCloth`); Chameleon (ID: `fauna:Chameleon`); Lizard (ID: `fauna:Lizard`); Monitor (ID: `fauna:Iguana`); Creeping Animal (ID: `fauna:CreepingAnimal`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`)
## Acts 11:18 (id: 9305)

Peter gave a clear and careful explanation. Those who had objected were convinced. They saw that God was at work in the Gentiles' believing in Jesus and in their receiving eternal life.

However, the question of including Gentiles was not finished. Soon it would lead to a serious crisis in the church ([15:1–35](https://ref.ly/Acts15:1-Acts15:35); see also Paul's letter to the Galatians).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`)
## Acts 11:19–26 (id: 9306)

* After Stephen's death, persecution forced Christians to move to other areas ([8:1–3](https://ref.ly/Acts8:1-Acts8:3)). They traveled as far as Phoenicia, Cyprus, and Antioch.
* Antioch in Syria was a busy, diverse city. It was the third\-largest city in the Roman Empire, after Rome and Alexandria. Antioch played a key role in spreading the Christian message to non\-Jewish people.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:19-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Antioch (ID: `place:Antioch`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cyprus (ID: `place:Cyprus`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Hellenists (ID: `group:Hellenist`); Phoenicia (ID: `place:Phoenicia`); Christians (ID: `group:Christ`); Cypriots (ID: `group:Cyprus`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Tarsus (ID: `place:Tarsus`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Greece (ID: `place:Greece`); Stephen (ID: `person:Stephen`); Cyrene (ID: `place:Cyrene`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`)

## Acts 11:21–24 (id: 9308)

As with the household of Cornelius, many Gentiles believed in Jesus. The growth of Christian faith among Gentiles had to remain in harmony with the church at Jerusalem. So, the Jerusalem Christians sent Barnabas to Antioch to oversee the developments there. Barnabas could see that God’s blessing was on what was happening, so he supported it with joy.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:21-24

## Acts 11:28 (id: 9312)

Claudius was the nephew of Tiberius Caesar ([Luke 3:1](https://ref.ly/Luke3:1)). He ruled as Roman emperor from AD 41 to AD 54\.

His last wife was his niece Agrippina. Claudius adopted her son, Nero.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:28

## Acts 11:29–30 (id: 9313)

Agabus's prophecy resulted in the Christians in Antioch helping the Jewish Christians in Judea. They gave as much as they could and trusted the funds to Barnabas and Saul. This act of love and support showed the difference Jesus had made in their lives. Saul (also called Paul) mentions this visit in his letter to the Galatians ([Galatians 2:1–10](https://ref.ly/Gal2:1-Gal2:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:29-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`)
## Acts 11:30 (id: 9314)

This is the first mention in the book of Acts of elders as officers in the Christian church (see also [14:23](https://ref.ly/Acts14:23); [15:2–23](https://ref.ly/Acts15:2-Acts15:23); [16:4](https://ref.ly/Acts16:4); [20:17–35](https://ref.ly/Acts20:17-Acts20:35); [21:18](https://ref.ly/Acts21:18); compare [1 Timothy 3:1–7](https://ref.ly/1Tim3:1-1Tim3:7); [Titus 1:5–9](https://ref.ly/Titus1:5-Titus1:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`)
## Acts 12:1–5 (id: 9316)

Jesus had clearly predicted persecution and hardship for his followers ([Luke 11:49–51](https://ref.ly/Luke11:49-Luke11:51)). For the first time since Jesus’ death, Roman authorities took direct violent action against the church. James*,* the brother of John, was one of the first called to be a disciple ([Mark 1:16–20](https://ref.ly/Mark1:16-Mark1:20); [Luke 5:1–11](https://ref.ly/Luke5:1-Luke5:11)), and he was one of the first Christians to be martyred for his faith. • King Herod Agrippa attacked the church (cp. [Acts 12:20–23](https://ref.ly/Acts12:20-Acts12:23)), a move that he found to be politically helpful with the Jewish people. The letter of James, the brother of Jesus, was probably written after this persecution to the scattered Christians (see [8:1–4](https://ref.ly/Acts8:1-Acts8:4); James Book Introduction, “Date of Writing”).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 12:1-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); James (ID: `person:James`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Herod (ID: `person:Herod.3`); Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)
## Acts 12:4 (id: 9321)

Herod had Peter guarded by four squads of soldiers, making it humanly impossible for the apostle to escape (cp. [12:6](https://ref.ly/Acts12:6)). However, God was in charge, and nothing is too hard for him (cp. [4:27–31](https://ref.ly/Acts4:27-Acts4:31); [Gen 18:14](https://ref.ly/Gen18:14); [Jer 32:17](https://ref.ly/Jer32:17), [27](https://ref.ly/Jer32:27); [Matt 19:26](https://ref.ly/Matt19:26); [Mark 10:27](https://ref.ly/Mark10:27); [Luke 1:37](https://ref.ly/Luke1:37); [18:27](https://ref.ly/Luke18:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 12:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

the church prayed very earnestly: God answers the earnest prayers of his people ([12:6–17](https://ref.ly/Acts12:6-Acts12:17); see [Luke 11:1–13](https://ref.ly/Luke11:1-Luke11:13); [18:1–8](https://ref.ly/Luke18:1-Luke18:8); cp. [Matt 7:7–11](https://ref.ly/Matt7:7-Matt7:11); [John 15:7](https://ref.ly/John15:7); [Phil 4:6–7](https://ref.ly/Phil4:6-Phil4:7); [Jas 5:16](https://ref.ly/Jas5:16); [1 Jn 3:22](https://ref.ly/1John3:22)).

## Acts 12:6–19 (id: 9323)

God, through an angel*,* led Peter out, reunited him with his praying friends, and sent him out to carry on the work of spreading the Good News. The message advanced despite determined opposition.

## Acts 12:12 (id: 9317)

The home of Mary, the mother of John Mark*,* was evidently a gathering place for believers. John Mark later became a missionary colleague of Barnabas and Saul ([12:25](https://ref.ly/Acts12:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 12:12

Rhoda was so surprised when Peter appeared that she left him standing at the closed door. Both she and the other believers were amazed by God’s answer to their prayers ([12:5](https://ref.ly/Acts12:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 12:13-17

## Acts 12:18–23 (id: 9319)

When Peter couldn’t be found after a careful search*,* Herod interrogated the guards and put them to death (cp. [16:27](https://ref.ly/Acts16:27)). However, Herod met his own painful end as a divine judgment on his conceit when he accepted the people’s worship. Josephus records the death of Herod Agrippa I in greater detail (Josephus, *Antiquities* 19\.8\.1–2\).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 12:18-23

## Acts 13:1 (id: 9333)

prophets and teachers: See “The Gift of Prophecy” Theme Note; see also [1 Cor 12:28–29](https://ref.ly/1Cor12:28-1Cor12:29); [Eph 4:11](https://ref.ly/Eph4:11). • The name Simeon suggests a Jewish background (see [Gen 29:33](https://ref.ly/Gen29:33); [Luke 2:25](https://ref.ly/Luke2:25); [3:30](https://ref.ly/Luke3:30)); he is also called “the black man”—he was probably of African descent. • Lucius is a Latin name; he came from Cyrene*,* the capital of Libya in North Africa. He was probably one of the preachers from Cyrene who had brought the Christian message to Antioch ([Acts 11:20](https://ref.ly/Acts11:20)). • Manaen had been brought up with King Herod Antipas; he was probably Luke’s source for insight into Antipas’s thoughts and actions (see [Luke 9:7–9](https://ref.ly/Luke9:7-Luke9:9)). • Barnabas and Saul are prominently featured in the subsequent narrative.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:1
* **Associated Articles:** The Gift of Prophecy (ID: `500390@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

## Acts 13:1–3 (id: 9325)

The prophets and teachers of the church at Antioch spent significant time in worship and prayer, earnestly seeking the Lord’s will as they fasted and opened themselves to divine direction. As they prayed, the Holy Spirit spoke to them, and they set apart Barnabas and Saul in clear recognition of God’s call for them to carry out a special work in his name. The believers’ inward journey in prayer and listening to God is matched by their outward journey in service, evangelism, and mighty works of healing and salvation.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Manaen (ID: `person:Manaen`); Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.4`); Antioch (ID: `place:Antioch`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Cyrene (ID: `place:Cyrene`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`)
## Acts 13:3 (id: 9340)

the men laid their hands on them: This solemn act was only done after more fasting and prayer*;* the Pastoral Epistles warn against laying hands on a person to appoint that person as a Christian leader without due care and diligence ([1 Tim 5:22](https://ref.ly/1Tim5:22)). At this point, Barnabas and Saul were sent . . . on their way as missionaries of the church at Antioch.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`)
## Acts 13:4 (id: 9345)

Barnabas and Saul’s first missionary journey was undertaken with a strong consciousness of the Holy Spirit as their guide. • Seleucia was Antioch’s ancient seaport*,* located about twelve miles (20 km) west of the city at the mouth of the Orontes River. • Their journey took them westward by sea to the island of Cyprus*,* which was Barnabas’s homeland ([4:36](https://ref.ly/Acts4:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Seleucia (ID: `place:Seleucia`); Cyprus (ID: `place:Cyprus`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`)
## Acts 13:5 (id: 9348)

Landing in the town of Salamis on the eastern end of Cyprus, they went to the Jewish synagogues (see study note on 9:2), where the Jews could hear and respond to the Christian message. They would also meet converts to Judaism (see study note on 13:43) and spiritually hungry Gentiles who are sometimes described as “God\-fearers” (see study note on 10:2).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:5
* **Associated Articles:** Acts 10:2 (ID: `9296@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Acts 13:43 (ID: `9343@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Acts 9:2 (ID: `9662@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Acts 13:6 (id: 9350)

Paphos was located on the southwest coast of Cyprus. As the leading city of Cyprus, it was of strategic importance. • a Jewish sorcerer . . . named Bar\-Jesus: Such eastern magicians often exercised a tremendous influence in the Greco\-Roman world.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bar-jesus (ID: `person:Bar-jesus`); Paphos (ID: `place:Paphos`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)
## Acts 13:6–12 (id: 9349)

At Paphos there was a power struggle with a false prophet*,* with the result that the power of God was manifested and the Roman governor became a believer.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:6-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bar-jesus (ID: `person:Bar-jesus`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Sergius Paulus (ID: `person:SergiusPaulus`); Paphos (ID: `place:Paphos`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Straightness (ID: `keyterm:Straightness`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)
## Acts 13:7–8 (id: 9351)

The governor, Sergius Paulus, who was an intelligent man*,* was attracted to the teaching of Barnabas and Saul. But Elymas (the sorcerer’s Greco\-Roman name) recognized a challenge to his power and strongly opposed the message of Barnabas and Saul.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:7-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sergius Paulus (ID: `person:SergiusPaulus`); Bar-jesus (ID: `person:Bar-jesus`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Acts 13:9 (id: 9352)

Luke makes the significant transition from the name Saul (a Hebrew name) to Paul (a Greco\-Roman name), perhaps indicating that Paul was now on a predominantly Gentile mission. For the rest of the book of Acts, he is called Paul except when he recounts his conversion (as in [22:7](https://ref.ly/Acts22:7), [13](https://ref.ly/Acts22:13); [26:14](https://ref.ly/Acts26:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`)
## Acts 13:10–11 (id: 9326)

Paul, who became the chief spokesman, rebuked the sorcerer’s fraudulent claims, exposed his deceit, and pronounced divine judgment (cp. [8:20–24](https://ref.ly/Acts8:20-Acts8:24)). The sorcerer was instantly struck blind*,* a condition that lasted for some time*,* giving a strong demonstration of the truthfulness and superiority of the apostolic message over the bogus claims of the sorcerer.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:10-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Straightness (ID: `keyterm:Straightness`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`)
## Acts 13:12 (id: 9327)

The teaching about the Lord included a miraculous demonstration of divine power (cp. [Mark 1:21–27](https://ref.ly/Mark1:21-Mark1:27)), for it was a teaching about the living God (see [Acts 14:15](https://ref.ly/Acts14:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Acts 13:13–14 (id: 9328)

They landed at the port of Perga. From here, major roads opened into the interior beyond the Taurus Mountains. • At this point, John Mark left the team for reasons that are not stated (see study note on 15:36–41). Possibly he was unhappy that the Good News was moving out into Gentile lands; possibly he was homesick or otherwise unable to continue a difficult journey. Whatever the reason, he returned to the more familiar and comfortable Jewish surroundings of Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:13-14

## Acts 13:14 (id: 9329)

As was their custom, the apostles began at the synagogue (see study note on 9:2; cp. [13:5](https://ref.ly/Acts13:5); [14:1](https://ref.ly/Acts14:1); [17:1](https://ref.ly/Acts17:1), [2](https://ref.ly/Acts17:2), [10](https://ref.ly/Acts17:10), [17](https://ref.ly/Acts17:17); [18:4](https://ref.ly/Acts18:4), [19](https://ref.ly/Acts18:19); [19:8](https://ref.ly/Acts19:8)).

## Acts 13:15 (id: 9330)

After the usual Scriptures for the day had been read, one from the books of Moses and the other from the prophets (see [Luke 4:16–28](https://ref.ly/Luke4:16-Luke4:28)), the officials of the synagogue invited the visitors to speak any word of encouragement for the people.

## Acts 13:16–41 (id: 9331)

Paul accepted the invitation, motioned to quiet his audience (cp. [19:33](https://ref.ly/Acts19:33); [21:40](https://ref.ly/Acts21:40)), and launched into a straightforward proclamation of the Good News. This is Paul’s first great speech in Acts, and it provides a model of his preaching to a Jewish audience (see [22:1–21](https://ref.ly/Acts22:1-Acts22:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:16-41

## Acts 13:23–25 (id: 9335)

Paul here moved to the theme of his message: Jesus*,* one of David’s descendants*,* was God’s promised Savior of Israel. ## Acts 13:26–37 (id: 9336)

Paul reviewed the shameful treatment Jesus had received, involving unjust condemnation and death. But God raised Jesus from the dead*,* as attested by witnesses. Paul appealed for them to believe the message about Jesus, through whom there is forgiveness for your sins.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:38-41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Law (ID: `keyterm:Law`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)
## Acts 13:39 (id: 9339)

Faith is prerequisite to being made right in God’s sight*;* this was not provided for in the law of Moses ([Ps 14:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps14:1-Ps14:3); see [Rom 3:9–20](https://ref.ly/Rom3:9-Rom3:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)
## Acts 13:40–41 (id: 9341)

Paul closed his message with a strong warning (cp. [Heb 2:3](https://ref.ly/Heb2:3)). The Good News must not be ignored, neglected, or rejected, or frightening consequences will follow.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:40-41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)
## Acts 13:42–43 (id: 9342)

The message stirred up interest among the people, and many of them were converted.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:42-43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Worship (ID: `keyterm:Worship`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)
## Acts 13:43 (id: 9343)

Full converts to Judaism (also called *proselytes*) were Gentiles who had gone through the rite of circumcision to become full members of the Jewish community, observing the Jewish law (see also [2:11](https://ref.ly/Acts2:11); [6:5](https://ref.ly/Acts6:5); [Matt 23:15](https://ref.ly/Matt23:15)). • The grace of God is a key concept in the New Testament to describe God’s unmerited favor shown preeminently through Jesus Christ.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Worship (ID: `keyterm:Worship`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)
## Acts 13:44–49 (id: 9344)

The excitement caused by the apostles’ preaching led to a mass turnout on the following week. This response provoked the jealousy of some of the Jews (cp. [4:1–2](https://ref.ly/Acts4:1-Acts4:2); [5:17](https://ref.ly/Acts5:17)), whose ability to win converts to Judaism ([13:43](https://ref.ly/Acts13:43)) was being dwarfed by Paul’s ministry. They verbally attacked Paul and his ministry (cp. [6:8–12](https://ref.ly/Acts6:8-Acts6:12); [18:6](https://ref.ly/Acts18:6); [19:9](https://ref.ly/Acts19:9); [Matt 23:13](https://ref.ly/Matt23:13)). Paul met this hostility with a bold declaration that these Jews had had their opportunity to hear the word of God*,* and that since they had rejected it, the offer of salvation would now be given to the Gentiles (cp. [Acts 10:34–35](https://ref.ly/Acts10:34-Acts10:35)), in accord with the Lord’s command in Scripture. The local Gentiles welcomed the Good News and many responded to it, so the Lord’s message was carried throughout that region.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:44-49

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Resentment (ID: `keyterm:Resentment`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Blaspheme (ID: `keyterm:Blaspheme`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)
## Acts 13:50–51 (id: 9346)

Jewish opposition once again forced Paul and Barnabas . . . out of town. They shook the dust from their feet as a sign of rejection*,* as Jesus had taught his disciples (see [Matt 10:14–15](https://ref.ly/Matt10:14-Matt10:15); [Mark 6:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark6:11-Mark6:12); [Luke 9:5–6](https://ref.ly/Luke9:5-Luke9:6); [10:10–11](https://ref.ly/Luke10:10-Luke10:11)). That place was then treated as pagan territory, and other people were given access to the message of new life in Christ.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:50-51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Worship (ID: `keyterm:Worship`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Iconium (ID: `place:Iconium`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)
## Acts 13:52 (id: 9347)

Despite probable harassment and persecution, the believers were filled with joy and with the Holy Spirit (cp. [5:41](https://ref.ly/Acts5:41); [16:23–25](https://ref.ly/Acts16:23-Acts16:25); [Matt 5:10–12](https://ref.ly/Matt5:10-Matt5:12); [2 Cor 8:2](https://ref.ly/2Cor8:2); [1 Thes 1:6](https://ref.ly/1Thess1:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:52

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Cause of Joy (ID: `keyterm:CauseOfJoy`)
## Acts 14:1 (id: 9356)

The missionaries moved on to the next town, Iconium (now called Konya), located east of Pisidian Antioch on a high plateau in south central Asia Minor. Iconium enjoyed a favorable location on several key trade routes. • In their usual fashion, Paul and Barnabas began their ministry in the area by speaking powerfully in the Jewish synagogue*,* which led to a great response from both Jews and Greeks.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Iconium (ID: `place:Iconium`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Greeks (ID: `group:Greek`); Javan (ID: `person:Javan`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)
## Acts 14:2 (id: 9360)

Once again Paul and Barnabas faced hostile Jews who rejected the Christian message and also poisoned the minds of the Gentiles.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)
## Acts 14:3 (id: 9361)

The Spirit\-inspired apostles were resilient to withstand the verbal attack against them, and they persisted in preaching the Good News that God’s grace was available to Gentiles as well as to Jews. • boldly: Spirit\-inspired boldness is evident throughout Acts (see [2:14](https://ref.ly/Acts2:14); [4:9–10](https://ref.ly/Acts4:9-Acts4:10), [13](https://ref.ly/Acts4:13); [7:2–53](https://ref.ly/Acts7:2-Acts7:53); [8:30–35](https://ref.ly/Acts8:30-Acts8:35); [9:27–28](https://ref.ly/Acts9:27-Acts9:28); [18:26](https://ref.ly/Acts18:26); [19:8](https://ref.ly/Acts19:8); [22:3–21](https://ref.ly/Acts22:3-Acts22:21); [23:1–6](https://ref.ly/Acts23:1-Acts23:6); [28:16–20](https://ref.ly/Acts28:16-Acts28:20), [23–31](https://ref.ly/Acts28:23-Acts28:31)). • In addition, the Holy Spirit confirmed the Christian message with miraculous signs and wonders (cp. [5:12–16](https://ref.ly/Acts5:12-Acts5:16); [15:12](https://ref.ly/Acts15:12); [16:18](https://ref.ly/Acts16:18); [19:11](https://ref.ly/Acts19:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Portent (ID: `keyterm:Portent`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`)
## Acts 14:4 (id: 9362)

The apostolic message required a decision about belief in Jesus, and opinion was clearly split. • Paul and Barnabas are called apostles for the first time (also in [14:14](https://ref.ly/Acts14:14)), extending the idea of apostleship beyond the Twelve. Their message was in continuity with that of the original apostles, and they were prepared to suffer hardship and persecution for it as the earlier apostles had done ([5:41](https://ref.ly/Acts5:41); [14:19](https://ref.ly/Acts14:19); [20:24](https://ref.ly/Acts20:24); [21:13–14](https://ref.ly/Acts21:13-Acts21:14); see also [Rom 8:35–38](https://ref.ly/Rom8:35-Rom8:38); [2 Cor 4:8–17](https://ref.ly/2Cor4:8-2Cor4:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)
## Acts 14:6 (id: 9363)

Lycaonia was a southern region of the Roman province of Galatia. Its major cities were Lystra, Derbe, Laranda, and Iconium. Acts reports two more visits by Paul to Lycaonia ([16:1–5](https://ref.ly/Acts16:1-Acts16:5); [18:23](https://ref.ly/Acts18:23)). Paul’s letter to the Galatians was probably addressed to scattered believers in the churches of Lycaonia (see Galatians Book Introduction, “Recipients”). • Lystra was located roughly twenty\-five miles (40 km) south\-southwest of Iconium. • Derbe*,* situated about thirty miles (50 km) southeast of Lystra, was on the busy major road that stretched from Iconium and Lystra eastward to Tarsus, the capital of Cilicia.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lycaonia (ID: `place:Lycaonia`); Derbe (ID: `place:Derbe`); Lystra (ID: `place:Lystra`)
## Acts 14:8–20 (id: 9364)

Paul’s healing of a man with crippled feet recalls a similar incident in [3:1–12](https://ref.ly/Acts3:1-Acts3:12). In Acts, the work of Paul parallels the work of Peter, and the many miraculous signs and wonders performed among the Jews were also performed among the Gentiles.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:8-20

## Acts 14:9 (id: 9365)

Paul realized he had faith to be healed: Paul, full of the Holy Spirit ([9:17](https://ref.ly/Acts9:17)), sensed the man’s spiritual openness and expectancy for divine help and intervention.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)
## Acts 14:11–13 (id: 9353)

Zeus was the patron god of Lystra, and the city had a temple in his honor. They associated Barnabas with Zeus, perhaps because he was the more impressive figure, and Hermes was identified with Paul . . . since he was the chief speaker. The native people of Lystra thought that they were being treated to a divine visitation similar to a past mythological appearance cited by the poet Ovid (in which the gods Zeus and Hermes came to visit the area but were unrecognized except by an old couple; see Ovid, *Metamorphoses* 8\.616–724\). So the people set out to honor these supposed gods.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:11-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zeus (ID: `deity:Zeus`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Lycaonians (ID: `group:Lycaonia`); Hermes (ID: `deity:Hermes`); Garland (ID: `realia:Garland`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Lycaonia (ID: `place:Lycaonia`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

The apostles opposed the people’s idolatry and their attempt to offer sacrifices to them. They directed the people’s worship to the living God*;* the apostles were his representatives as they brought the Good News of the Christian message.

The crowd became fickle when some Jews arrived from Antioch and Iconium and easily turned the people against the apostles. • They stoned Paul and dragged him out of town: Paul later referred to this time of persecution as a lesson ([2 Tim 3:11–12](https://ref.ly/2Tim3:11-2Tim3:12)). • thinking he was dead: That Paul got up and went back into the town suggests that God miraculously healed him of his wounds.

## Acts 14:22–23 (id: 9358)

The apostles were diligent in following up with those who had made a Christian profession of faith. These new disciples needed to be nurtured, supported, and encouraged ([15:32](https://ref.ly/Acts15:32), [41](https://ref.ly/Acts15:41); [16:40](https://ref.ly/Acts16:40); [18:23](https://ref.ly/Acts18:23); see [1 Thes 3:2](https://ref.ly/1Thess3:2); [4:18](https://ref.ly/1Thess4:18); [5:14](https://ref.ly/1Thess5:14)). • Paul and Barnabas … appointed elders: See “Church Leaders” Theme Note.

## Acts 14:26–28 (id: 9359)

As soon as Paul and Barnabas returned home to Antioch from their first missionary journey, they called the church together and gave a full report of their evangelism and discipleship. They humbly acknowledged divine guidance in opening the door of faith to the Gentiles (see [11:18](https://ref.ly/Acts11:18); [1 Cor 16:9](https://ref.ly/1Cor16:9); [2 Cor 2:12](https://ref.ly/2Cor2:12)). Similar reports ([Acts 15:4](https://ref.ly/Acts15:4), [12](https://ref.ly/Acts15:12); [21:19](https://ref.ly/Acts21:19)) always stressed the activity of God as working through the ministries of his servants (see [Rom 15:17–18](https://ref.ly/Rom15:17-Rom15:18); [1 Cor 3:5–9](https://ref.ly/1Cor3:5-1Cor3:9); [15:10–11](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:10-1Cor15:11)).

## Acts 15:1 (id: 9369)

These men from Judea were Jewish Christians who taught the necessity of circumcision (and with it, conversion to Judaism) in order to be saved. The central issue was how Gentiles were to be received into Christian fellowship. Paul wrote his letter to the Galatians about this time to counter the same teaching in Galatia (see Galatians Book Introduction, “Date of Writing”).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 15:1

The Antioch church decided to send . . . delegates to Jerusalem to resolve this matter in discussion with the apostles and elders there. En route, the entourage visited believers in Phoenicia and Samaria*,* where the Christian faith had made substantial inroads (ch [8](https://ref.ly/Acts8:1-Acts8:40)).

## Acts 15:20 (id: 9371)

Eating food offered to idols is sinful if it involves knowingly partaking of an idolatrous sacrifice (see [Exod 20:4](https://ref.ly/Exod20:4); [Deut 5:8](https://ref.ly/Deut5:8); [1 Cor 8:4–13](https://ref.ly/1Cor8:4-1Cor8:13); [10:14–30](https://ref.ly/1Cor10:14-1Cor10:30); [Rev 2:14](https://ref.ly/Rev2:14), [20](https://ref.ly/Rev2:20)). • Sexual immorality was common in the Greek world but is always sinful ([Exod 20:14](https://ref.ly/Exod20:14); [Deut 5:18](https://ref.ly/Deut5:18); [Gal 5:19](https://ref.ly/Gal5:19)). • eating the meat of strangled animals: God’s law prohibits eating meat that has blood in it ([Lev 17:13–14](https://ref.ly/Lev17:13-Lev17:14)) or consuming blood ([Lev 17:10–12](https://ref.ly/Lev17:10-Lev17:12)), “for the life of every creature is in its blood” ([Lev 17:14](https://ref.ly/Lev17:14)). Also, “It is the blood, given in exchange for a life, that makes purification possible” (see [Lev 17:11](https://ref.ly/Lev17:11) and study note). This command was first given to Noah, the ancestor of Gentiles as well as Jews ([Gen 9:4](https://ref.ly/Gen9:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 15:20
* **Associated Articles:** Leviticus 17:11 (ID: `20167@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Fornication (ID: `keyterm:Fornication`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)
## Acts 15:22–29 (id: 9372)

The Jerusalem church chose two of the church leaders to report its decision. They took with them a letter from the apostles and elders . . . in Jerusalem explaining the terms of the agreement.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 15:22-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judas (ID: `person:Judas.5`); Silas (ID: `person:Silas`); Chosen (ID: `keyterm:Chosen`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Antioch (ID: `place:Antioch`); Fornication (ID: `keyterm:Fornication`); Cilicia (ID: `place:Cilicia`); Beloved (ID: `keyterm:Beloved`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`)
## Acts 15:29 (id: 9373)

Farewell: The letter to the churches includes this familiar Hellenistic greeting at the end of a letter, found only here in the New Testament. Paul often ended his letters on a more theological note (e.g., [1 Cor 16:23–24](https://ref.ly/1Cor16:23-1Cor16:24); [2 Cor 13:13](https://ref.ly/2Cor13:13); see [Eph 6:23–24](https://ref.ly/Eph6:23-Eph6:24); [Phil 4:23](https://ref.ly/Phil4:23); [Col 4:18](https://ref.ly/Col4:18); [1 Thes 5:23–28](https://ref.ly/1Thess5:23-1Thess5:28); [1 Tim 6:21](https://ref.ly/1Tim6:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 15:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Fornication (ID: `keyterm:Fornication`)
## Acts 15:30–31 (id: 9374)

The believers at Antioch received the decision with great joy that conversion to Judaism and keeping all the requirements of the law of Moses was not required of them. The dispute had been resolved, so the work of teaching and preaching in Antioch could continue unimpeded.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 15:30-31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Antioch (ID: `place:Antioch`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)
## Acts 15:36–41 (id: 9375)

The discordant separation of Paul and Barnabas is indicated by a very strong word in Greek (their disagreement was so sharp). It was an honest disagreement between two godly men about whether John Mark (Barnabas’s cousin, [Col 4:10](https://ref.ly/Col4:10)) should be given a second chance to accompany them on a mission journey after he had deserted them in Pamphylia (see [Acts 13:13](https://ref.ly/Acts13:13)). The rift between Paul and John Mark was eventually healed ([2 Tim 4:11](https://ref.ly/2Tim4:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 15:36-41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Mark (ID: `person:Mark`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cilicia (ID: `place:Cilicia`); Pamphylia (ID: `place:Pamphylia`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Silas (ID: `person:Silas`); Cyprus (ID: `place:Cyprus`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`)
## Acts 15:40–41 (id: 9377)

Following his disagreement with Barnabas over John Mark, Paul chose Silas as his partner for a second missionary journey through Syria and Cilicia. The two men traveled overland to visit Derbe and Lystra, explaining the decision of the Jerusalem council and strengthening believers in their faith.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 15:40-41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cilicia (ID: `place:Cilicia`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Silas (ID: `person:Silas`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`)
## Acts 16:1–3 (id: 9380)

Paul returned to churches that he and Barnabas had established on their previous journey.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lystra (ID: `place:Lystra`); Greeks (ID: `group:Greek`); Javan (ID: `person:Javan`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Derbe (ID: `place:Derbe`); Timothy (ID: `person:Timothy`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Faithfulness (ID: `keyterm:Faithfulness`); Iconium (ID: `place:Iconium`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`)
## Acts 16:3 (id: 9397)

Paul had Timothy circumcised to enhance Timothy’s acceptance and effectiveness as a Jew in Jewish circles (contrast Titus, who was a full Gentile, [Gal 2:3](https://ref.ly/Gal2:3)). Elsewhere Paul clarified that it makes no difference to God whether one is circumcised or not circumcised ([Rom 2:25–29](https://ref.ly/Rom2:25-Rom2:29); [Gal 5:6](https://ref.ly/Gal5:6); [6:15](https://ref.ly/Gal6:15)). Paul was prepared to use any legitimate means to communicate the Good News ([1 Cor 9:20–21](https://ref.ly/1Cor9:20-1Cor9:21)) to various audiences.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Javan (ID: `person:Javan`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Greeks (ID: `group:Greek`)
## Acts 16:4–5 (id: 9398)

Paul and Silas faithfully communicated the decisions of the Jerusalem council. The wisdom of the decision was indicated as the churches were strengthened in their faith and grew larger every day (cp. [2:41](https://ref.ly/Acts2:41); [4:4](https://ref.ly/Acts4:4); [5:14](https://ref.ly/Acts5:14); [6:1](https://ref.ly/Acts6:1); [9:31](https://ref.ly/Acts9:31); [21:20](https://ref.ly/Acts21:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`)
## Acts 16:6–10 (id: 9400)

God directed the missionaries’ travels: The Holy Spirit had prevented them from proceeding westward into the province of Asia, the Spirit of Jesus did not allow them to go north to Bithynia*,* and then Paul had a vision calling them to go northwest over the Aegean Sea to Macedonia. God’s Spirit guided his servants in Acts in a variety of ways, including divine visions (see also [9:10](https://ref.ly/Acts9:10); [10:9–16](https://ref.ly/Acts10:9-Acts10:16); [22:18](https://ref.ly/Acts22:18)), direct intuition (cp. [8:29](https://ref.ly/Acts8:29), [39](https://ref.ly/Acts8:39); [10:19](https://ref.ly/Acts10:19); [20:22](https://ref.ly/Acts20:22)), counsel with other believers (cp. [15:29](https://ref.ly/Acts15:29)), guidance through prayer ([13:2](https://ref.ly/Acts13:2), [4](https://ref.ly/Acts13:4)), insight through Scripture ([28:25–27](https://ref.ly/Acts28:25-Acts28:27)), and prophecy ([11:28](https://ref.ly/Acts11:28)). Guidance by the indwelling Holy Spirit helped them to accomplish their mission to be Christ’s witnesses ([1:8](https://ref.ly/Acts1:8); see also [4:8](https://ref.ly/Acts4:8), [31](https://ref.ly/Acts4:31); [11:24](https://ref.ly/Acts11:24); [13:9](https://ref.ly/Acts13:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:6-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mysia (ID: `place:Mysia`); Macedonia (ID: `place:Macedonia`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Troas (ID: `place:Troas`); Asia (ID: `place:Asia`); Galatia (ID: `place:Galatia`); Phrygia (ID: `place:Phrygia`); Bithynia (ID: `place:Bithynia`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)
## Acts 16:8 (id: 9401)

Troas, a major port on the Aegean Sea, was one of the more significant cities in the Roman Empire. Paul’s strategy was often to visit principal places such as Troas, Athens, Corinth, and Ephesus. When the Christian faith had been planted in major centers, local Christian workers could carry it into outlying areas (e.g., Epaphras took the message from Ephesus to Colosse, [Col 1:7](https://ref.ly/Col1:7); [4:12–13](https://ref.ly/Col4:12-Col4:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Troas (ID: `place:Troas`); Mysia (ID: `place:Mysia`)
## Acts 16:9–10 (id: 9402)

a vision: See “Visions” Theme Note.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:9-10
* **Associated Articles:** Visions (ID: `500385@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Macedonia (ID: `place:Macedonia`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Acts 16:10 (id: 9381)

we: The most natural interpretation of the shift from “they” to “we” is that Luke accompanied Paul from Troas to Philippi ([16:10–17](https://ref.ly/Acts16:10-Acts16:17)). Later Luke joined Paul again at Philippi and sailed with him to Troas, then to Miletus ([20:5–15](https://ref.ly/Acts20:5-Acts20:15)), then from Miletus to Jerusalem ([21:1–18](https://ref.ly/Acts21:1-Acts21:18)). After Paul’s two\-year imprisonment in Caesarea, Luke traveled with him to Rome ([27:1–28:16](https://ref.ly/Acts27:1-Acts28:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Macedonia (ID: `place:Macedonia`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`)
## Acts 16:11 (id: 9382)

Samothrace is a small mountainous island lying west\-northwest of the Hellespont about twenty miles (32 km) from the coast of Thrace. • Neapolis (modern Kavala) was the seaport for Philippi and the eastern terminus of the famous *Via Egnatia* (the Egnatian Way), which ran from Rome to Asia.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Samothrace (ID: `place:Samothrace`); Neapolis (ID: `place:Neapolis`); Troas (ID: `place:Troas`)
## Acts 16:12 (id: 9383)

At Philippi*,* about ten miles (17 km) inland from Neapolis, Paul began his missionary labors in Europe.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Philippi (ID: `place:Philippi`); Macedonia (ID: `place:Macedonia`); Rome (ID: `place:Rome`)
## Acts 16:13 (id: 9385)

The Jewish community at Philippi was too small to have a synagogue, which required ten adult males. Instead, Jews met for prayer in an open space by the Gangites (now called Angista) River that afforded privacy, quiet, and water for Jewish purification rites.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)
## Acts 16:13–36 (id: 9384)

Luke gives his readers cameos of three lives touched by the Good News in Philippi: a wealthy woman ([16:14–15](https://ref.ly/Acts16:14-Acts16:15)), an exploited slave girl ([16:16–21](https://ref.ly/Acts16:16-Acts16:21)), and a middle\-class officer ([16:23–36](https://ref.ly/Acts16:23-Acts16:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:13-36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); Silas (ID: `person:Silas`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Door (ID: `realia:Door`); Philippian Jailer (ID: `person:PhilippianJailer`); Thyatira (ID: `place:Thyatira`); Lydia (ID: `person:Lydia`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Faithfulness (ID: `keyterm:Faithfulness`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Worship (ID: `keyterm:Worship`); Rome (ID: `place:Rome`); Torch (ID: `realia:Torch`); Chain (ID: `realia:Chain`); Foundation (ID: `realia:Foundation`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`)
## Acts 16:14–15 (id: 9386)

The first person changed by Christ in Philippi was Lydia*,* a successful businesswoman from Thyatira (modern Akhisar), a city of western Asia Minor famous for its woolen fabrics, weavers, and linens. Lydia responded to the message and she and her household were baptized (cp. [16:32–33](https://ref.ly/Acts16:32-Acts16:33)). • One of Lydia’s first acts as a true believer in the Lord was to extend hospitality to the visiting missionaries. Hospitality is an important Christian virtue ([Matt 25:31–46](https://ref.ly/Matt25:31-Matt25:46); [Rom 12:13](https://ref.ly/Rom12:13); [16:23](https://ref.ly/Rom16:23); [1 Tim 3:2](https://ref.ly/1Tim3:2); [Titus 1:8](https://ref.ly/Titus1:8); [Heb 13:2](https://ref.ly/Heb13:2); [1 Pet 4:9](https://ref.ly/1Pet4:9); [3 Jn 1:5–8](https://ref.ly/3John1:5-3John1:8); cp. [Gen 18:1–8](https://ref.ly/Gen18:1-Gen18:8); [19:1–3](https://ref.ly/Gen19:1-Gen19:3); [24:23–33](https://ref.ly/Gen24:23-Gen24:33)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:14-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Lydia (ID: `person:Lydia`); Thyatira (ID: `place:Thyatira`); Worship (ID: `keyterm:Worship`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Faithfulness (ID: `keyterm:Faithfulness`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)
## Acts 16:16–18 (id: 9387)

The second portrait of a changed life in Philippi is of a slave girl who had a spirit that enabled her to tell the future. • Even though the demon within her was stating the truth, Paul, like Jesus, did not permit it to proclaim the Christian message (cp. [Mark 1:25](https://ref.ly/Mark1:25), [34](https://ref.ly/Mark1:34); [3:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11-Mark3:12); [Luke 4:35](https://ref.ly/Luke4:35), [41](https://ref.ly/Luke4:41)). The Lord had commanded that the gospel be proclaimed by his disciples, not by opponents ([Acts 1:8](https://ref.ly/Acts1:8); [9:15](https://ref.ly/Acts9:15); [26:15–18](https://ref.ly/Acts26:15-Acts26:18); [Matt 28:18–20](https://ref.ly/Matt28:18-Matt28:20); [Luke 24:46–49](https://ref.ly/Luke24:46-Luke24:49); [John 20:21](https://ref.ly/John20:21); see[Acts 22:15](https://ref.ly/Acts22:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:16-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)
## Acts 16:19–21 (id: 9388)

As in the ministry of Jesus, sometimes the Good News threatened established commercial interests (see also [19:25–27](https://ref.ly/Acts19:25-Acts19:27); [Mark 5:1–20](https://ref.ly/Mark5:1-Mark5:20)). The slave girl’s exploiters viciously attacked the missionaries and dragged them before the authorities as criminals, blamed them for fomenting a disturbance, resorted to racial bias (these Jews), and appealed to the Philippians’ pride (us Romans*;* the Philippians prided themselves on being Roman citizens of a Roman colony). • customs that are illegal: By law, Jews were not permitted to make converts of Romans.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:19-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`); Rome (ID: `place:Rome`); Silas (ID: `person:Silas`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)
## Acts 16:22–24 (id: 9389)

All reasonable security measures were taken to ensure that Paul and Silas didn’t escape after they had been stripped and beaten with wooden rods (see [22:24–26](https://ref.ly/Acts22:24-Acts22:26); [2 Cor 6:5](https://ref.ly/2Cor6:5); [11:23–25](https://ref.ly/2Cor11:23-2Cor11:25)). As at Christ’s tomb ([Matt 27:65](https://ref.ly/Matt27:65)), however, human effort did not prevent divine intervention.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:22-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`)
## Acts 16:25 (id: 9390)

Paul and Silas*,* like the persecuted apostles in Jerusalem, were joyful, “rejoicing that God had counted them worthy to suffer disgrace for the name of Jesus” ([5:41](https://ref.ly/Acts5:41)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Silas (ID: `person:Silas`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`)
## Acts 16:26 (id: 9391)

The massive earthquake is reminiscent of the great earthquake at Jesus’ resurrection ([Matt 28:2–3](https://ref.ly/Matt28:2-Matt28:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Chain (ID: `realia:Chain`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); Door (ID: `realia:Door`); Foundation (ID: `realia:Foundation`)
## Acts 16:27 (id: 9393)

The Roman jailer clearly knew that his life could be forfeited if the prisoners for whom he was responsible escaped. This was standard practice ([12:19](https://ref.ly/Acts12:19); [27:42](https://ref.ly/Acts27:42); cp. *Code of Justinian* 9\.4\.4\).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); Door (ID: `realia:Door`)
## Acts 16:27–36 (id: 9392)

Luke’s third portrait in Philippi is of the Philippian jailer who, shaken by what had happened, responded in faith when challenged to accept the Lord Jesus and be saved. He was baptized with his household and rejoiced in his newfound faith as he reached out with hospitality to the preachers.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:27-36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Philippian Jailer (ID: `person:PhilippianJailer`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Silas (ID: `person:Silas`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Torch (ID: `realia:Torch`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Door (ID: `realia:Door`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)
## Acts 16:29–30 (id: 9394)

Paul and Silas had impressed the jailer with their cheerful faith ([16:25](https://ref.ly/Acts16:25)), their composure in crisis, and their concern for his well\-being ([16:28](https://ref.ly/Acts16:28)). Whatever the jailer’s previous understanding of Paul’s message, God touched his heart, and he cried out for divine help.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:29-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Torch (ID: `realia:Torch`); Silas (ID: `person:Silas`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)
## Acts 16:31–34 (id: 9395)

Paul and Silas directed the jailer to a faith in Christ that brings blessing both to him and to his family. The whole household received Christian instruction, and their response was expressed in baptism and the offering of hospitality to Paul and Silas.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:31-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)
## Acts 16:37–39 (id: 9396)

Paul and Silas were both Roman citizens*,* so the beating and imprisonment ([16:22–24](https://ref.ly/Acts16:22-Acts16:24)) had been illegal. After the city officials learned of this, they were justifiably alarmed because they had committed a crime against Rome. • Let them come themselves to release us: Paul made use of his rights as a Roman citizen in order to clear his name and ensure that no disrepute would attach to the Christian message or those who accepted it. Luke intended to show that Christianity should enjoy the same status as Judaism in the Roman Empire as a permitted religion—the Christian faith was compatible with the life of a Roman citizen. Accordingly, it was important to note that Paul made use of his rights as a Roman citizen (cp. [22:25–27](https://ref.ly/Acts22:25-Acts22:27)) and was ready to use the privileges of citizenship to advance the cause of Christ in a hostile world.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:37-39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rome (ID: `place:Rome`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)
## Acts 16:40 (id: 9399)

This meeting in the home of Lydia bolstered the spirits of the Philippian Christians who had to deal with the fallout from the missionaries’ work there.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:40

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lydia (ID: `person:Lydia`); Encourage (ID: `keyterm:Encourage`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)
## Acts 17:1–3 (id: 9403)

Amphipolis was a Roman military post located on the Egnatian Way in the northeastern part of Macedonia. • Apollonia*,* named after the Greek god Apollo, was also situated on the Egnatian Way. • Thessalonica offered a starting point for ministry because it had a Jewish synagogue (see study note on 9:2). Paul was able to preach for three consecutive Sabbaths*,* explaining the Scriptures and showing their fulfillment in Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 17:1-3
* **Associated Articles:** Acts 9:2 (ID: `9662@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Acts 17:1–9 (id: 9404)

After traveling through Macedonia to Thessalonica*,* Paul preached to the Jews first ([Rom 1:16](https://ref.ly/Rom1:16)) in the synagogue. Here, as elsewhere, there was a mixed response.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 17:1-9

## Acts 17:4 (id: 9414)

God\-fearing Greek men: See study note on 10:2. • Luke regularly draws attention to women who joined the Christian movement ([17:4](https://ref.ly/Acts17:4), [12](https://ref.ly/Acts17:12), [34](https://ref.ly/Acts17:34); see also [5:14](https://ref.ly/Acts5:14); [8:12](https://ref.ly/Acts8:12); [16:13–15](https://ref.ly/Acts16:13-Acts16:15), [31–34](https://ref.ly/Acts16:31-Acts16:34); [18:26](https://ref.ly/Acts18:26); [21:9](https://ref.ly/Acts21:9)).

## Acts 17:5–7 (id: 9415)

Once again, the enemies of the Christian faith saw it as a disruptive threat (cp. [16:19–21](https://ref.ly/Acts16:19-Acts16:21)). Ironically, they gathered some troublemakers and then accused Paul and Silas of causing trouble by disturbing the peace and committing treason against Caesar—serious charges of threatening the stability of the empire (cp. [Luke 23:2](https://ref.ly/Luke23:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 17:5-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jason (ID: `person:Jason`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Claudius (ID: `person:Claudius`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); House (ID: `realia:House`)
## Acts 17:8–9 (id: 9416)

The charges did not hold up under scrutiny, so the officials released Jason and the other believers after they posted bond. Christianity, Luke contended, was politically harmless to the Roman Empire and should therefore be recognized as a permitted religion and not subjected to political attack.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 17:8-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jason (ID: `person:Jason`)
## Acts 17:10–12 (id: 9405)

In light of the strong opposition in Thessalonica, the believers sent Paul and Silas to Berea*,* located about fifty miles (80\.5 km) west of Thessalonica. There the missionaries had a better reception than in Thessalonica. Many Jews came to faith, as well as many of the prominent Greek women and men. The Bereans were exemplary in their attitude, for they were open\-minded and eager to learn, good listeners, diligent Bible students, and thoughtful people. Their resulting faith had a strong foundation.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 17:10-12

Athens*,* like Alexandria and Tarsus, prided itself on its intellectual sophistication in examining ideas and considering the different philosophies that were current at the time.
